# PENAL CODE

PENAL CODE

(NOTE 1: See the law of 10 July 1996 abolishing the death penalty and amending criminal sentences, in particular article 3, 1996-07-10/42)
(NOTE: Consultation of previous versions from 05-04-1990 and updated to 29-10-2018)

## BOOK 1. - OFFENCES AND PUNISHMENT IN GENERAL

#### CHAPTER I. - OFFENCES

###### Article 1

An offence that the laws punish with a criminal penalty is a crime. An offence that the laws punish with a correctional penalty is an offence.
An offence that the laws punish with a police penalty is a petty offence.

###### Art. 2

No offence may be punished with penalties that were not provided for by law before the offence was committed.
If the penalty established at the time of judgment differs from that which was provided for at the time of the offence, the lesser penalty shall be applied.

###### Art. 3

An offence committed on the territory of the kingdom, by Belgians or by foreigners, is punished in accordance with the provisions of Belgian laws.

###### Art. 4

An offence committed outside the territory of the kingdom, by Belgians or by foreigners, is punished in Belgium only in the cases determined by law.

###### Art. 5

[1 Any legal person is criminally liable for offences that are intrinsically linked to the achievement of its object or to the defence of its interests, or for those whose concrete facts demonstrate that they were committed on its behalf.
The following are assimilated to legal persons:
1° temporary partnerships and internal partnerships;
2° companies referred to in article 2, § 4, paragraph 2, of the Companies Code, as well as companies in formation;
3° civil companies that have not taken the form of a commercial company.

The criminal liability of legal persons does not exclude that of natural persons who are authors of the same acts or who participated in them.]1

(1)<L 2018-07-11/10, art. 2, 132; In force: 30-07-2018>

###### Art. 6

The courts and tribunals shall continue to apply particular laws and regulations in all matters not regulated by this Code.

#### CHAPTER II. - PENALTIES

##### SECTION I. - THE VARIOUS TYPES OF PENALTIES

###### Art. 7

The penalties applicable to offences (committed by natural persons) are: <L 1999-05-04/60, art. 3, 024; In force: 02-07-1999> In criminal matters:
(1° imprisonment for serious offences;
2° detention.) <L 1996-07-10/42, art. 4, 018; In force: 11-08-1996> [2 In correctional and police matters:
1° imprisonment;
2° the penalty of electronic monitoring; 3° the work penalty;
4° the autonomous probation penalty.
The penalties provided for in 1° to 4° may not be applied cumulatively.]2 In criminal and correctional matters:
1° Deprivation of certain political and civil rights;
2° [1 placement at the disposal of the sentence implementation court;]1 In criminal, correctional and police matters:
1° The fine;
2° Special confiscation.

(1)<L 2007-04-26/89, art. 2, 069; In force: 01-01-2012>
(2)<L 2014-04-10/80, art. 2, 111; In force: indeterminate and at the latest 01-05-2016 (see L 2014-05-08/55, art. 6, itself modified by L 2015-11-23/02, art. 13), modified in the same sense by L 2016-02-05/11, art. 48, 114>

Art. 7bis.<Inserted by L 1999-05-04/60, art. 4; In force: 02-07-1999> [1 The

penalties applicable to offences committed by legal persons, with the exception of legal persons under public law referred to in paragraph 3, are:]1 in criminal, correctional and police matters:
1° the fine;
2° special confiscation; special confiscation provided for in article 42, 1°, pronounced against legal persons under public law, may only relate to civilly seizable property;
in criminal and correctional matters:
1° dissolution; this may not be pronounced against legal persons under public law;
2° prohibition from exercising an activity falling within the corporate object, with the exception of activities falling within a public service mission;
3° closure of one or more establishments, with the exception of establishments where activities falling within a public service mission are carried out;
4° publication or dissemination of the decision.
[1 With regard to the Federal State, the Regions, the Communities, the provinces, the emergency zones, the pre-zones, the Brussels Agglomeration, the municipalities, the multi-municipal zones, the intra-municipal territorial bodies, the French Community Commission, the Flemish Community Commission, the Joint Community Commission and the public social welfare centres, only a simple declaration of guilt may be pronounced, to the exclusion of any other penalty.]1

##### SECTION II. - CRIMINAL PENALTIES

###### Art. 8

<L 1996-07-10/42, art. 5, 018; In force: 11-08-1996> Imprisonment for serious offences is for life or for a fixed term.

###### Art. 9

<L 1996-07-10/42, art. 6, 018; In force: 11-08-1996> Imprisonment for serious offences for a fixed term is pronounced for a term of:
1° five to ten years;
2° ten to fifteen years;
3° fifteen to twenty years; 4° twenty to thirty years.

[1 5° thirty to forty years.]1

<L 1996-07-10/42, art. 7, 018; In force: 11-08-1996> Detention is for life or for a fixed term.

###### Art. 11

<L 1996-07-10/42, art. 8, 018; In force: 11-08-1996> Detention for a fixed term is pronounced for a term of:

2° ten to fifteen years;

##### SECTION III. - CORRECTIONAL IMPRISONMENT

###### Art. 25

[1 The duration of correctional imprisonment is, except in cases provided for by law, at least eight days and at most five years.
It is at most five years in the case of a crime punishable by imprisonment of five to ten years which has been correctionalized.
It is at most ten years in the case of a crime punishable by imprisonment of ten to fifteen years which has been correctionalized.
It is at most fifteen years in the case of a crime punishable by imprisonment of

fifteen to twenty years which has been correctionalized.
[2 It is at most twenty-eight years in the case of a crime punishable by imprisonment of twenty to thirty years which has been correctionalized.
It is at most thirty-eight years in the case of a crime punishable by imprisonment of thirty to forty years which has been correctionalized.
It is at most forty years in the case of a crime punishable by life imprisonment which has been correctionalized.]2]1
The duration of one day of imprisonment is twenty-four hours. The duration of one month of imprisonment is thirty days.

###### Subsection II. - (Penalties common to crimes and offences applicable to legal persons)

<Inserted by L 1999-05-04/60, art. 6; In force: 02-07-1999>

###### Art. 35

<L 1999-05-04/60, art. 6, 024; In force: 02-07-1999> Dissolution may be decided by the judge when the legal person was intentionally created in order to exercise the punishable activities for which it is convicted or when its object was intentionally diverted in order to exercise such activities.
When it decides dissolution, the judge refers the case to the competent court to hear the liquidation of the legal person.

###### Art. 36

<L 1999-05-04/60, art. 6, 024; In force: 02-07-1999> The temporary or permanent prohibition from exercising an activity falling within the corporate object of the legal person may be imposed by the judge in the cases provided for by law.

###### Art. 37

<L 1999-05-04/60, art. 6, 024; In force: 02-07-1999> The temporary or permanent closure of one or more establishments of the legal person may be imposed by the judge in the cases provided for by law.

Art. 37bis. <Inserted by L 1999-05-04/60, art. 6; In force: 02-07-1999> The publication or dissemination of the decision at the expense of the convicted person may be imposed by the judge in the cases determined by law.

SECTION Vbis. ([2 former section Vter renumbered as new section Vbis]2) [1 - On the penalty of electronic monitoring]1

(1)	<Inserted by L 2014-02-07/15, art. 6, 110; In force: undetermined and at the latest on 01-05-2016>
(2)	<L 2014-04-10/80, art. 3, 111; In force: undetermined and at the latest on 01-05-2016 (see L 2014-05-08/55, art. 6, itself modified by L 2015-11-23/02, art. 13)>

Art. 37ter.[

###### §

1. When an act is of a nature to entail a penalty of imprisonment of one year maximum, the judge may sentence as a principal penalty to a penalty of electronic monitoring of a duration equal to the penalty of imprisonment that he would have imposed and which may be applicable in case of non-execution of the penalty of electronic monitoring. For the determination of the duration of this subsidiary imprisonment penalty, one day of the penalty of electronic monitoring imposed corresponds to one day of imprisonment penalty.
A penalty of electronic monitoring consists of the obligation to be present at a specified address, with the exception of authorized movements or absences, during a period fixed by the judge in accordance with paragraph 2. Monitoring of presence is ensured in particular by recourse to electronic means and, in accordance with paragraph 5, this obligation is subject to conditions.
The penalty of electronic monitoring may not be imposed for acts: 1° referred to in articles 375 to 377;
2° referred to in articles 379 to 387, if the acts were committed on minors or with the assistance of minors;
3° referred to in articles 393 to 39

###### 7. §

2. The duration of the electronic monitoring sentence may not be less than one month nor more than one year. In accordance with Article 85, the criminal judge may take into account mitigating circumstances without, however, the fixed duration of electronic monitoring as an autonomous sentence being less than one month.
The electronic monitoring sentence [2 must begin]2 within six months following the date on which the judicial decision became final. If the exceeding of this period is attributable to the convicted person, the public prosecutor decides either to postpone the execution of the electronic monitoring sentence again, or to proceed with the execution of the subsidiary imprisonment. If the exceeding of this period is not attributable to the convicted person, the sentence must be

executed within six months following the expiry of the first period, failing which it is time-barred.

###### § 3

With a view to the application of an electronic monitoring sentence, the public prosecutor, the investigating judge, the investigating courts or the trial courts may instruct the competent service for the organisation and control of electronic monitoring, hereinafter referred to as "competent service for electronic monitoring", of the judicial district of the place of residence of the accused, the defendant or the convicted person to carry out a brief information report and/or a social investigation.
This report or this investigation contains only the relevant elements likely to inform the authority which addressed the request to the competent service for electronic monitoring on the appropriateness of the envisaged sentence.
Any adult person with whom the defendant cohabits is heard in their observations within the framework of this social investigation. The brief information report or the social investigation report is attached to the file within one month of the request.

###### § 4

When an electronic monitoring sentence is envisaged by the judge, requested by the public prosecutor or sought by the defendant, the judge informs the latter, before the close of the proceedings, of the scope of such a sentence, provides possible indications as to the concrete content he may give and as to the individualised conditions he may impose in accordance with paragraph 5 and hears him in his observations. The judge may also take into account, in this regard, the interests of any victims. The judge may only pronounce the electronic monitoring sentence if the defendant is present or represented at the hearing and after he has given, either in person or through his counsel, his consent. Any adult cohabitant of the defendant who has not been heard within the framework of the social investigation, or if no social investigation has been carried out, may be heard by the judge in their observations.
The judge who refuses to pronounce an electronic monitoring sentence requested by the public prosecutor or sought by the defendant must give reasons for his decision.

###### § 5

The judge determines the duration of the electronic monitoring sentence and may give indications as to its concrete modalities.
The electronic monitoring sentence is always subject to the following general conditions:
1° not to commit offences;
2° to have a fixed address and, in the event of a change of address, to communicate

without delay the address of his new residence to the public prosecutor and to the competent service for electronic monitoring;
3° to respond to summonses from the competent service for electronic monitoring and to comply with the concrete modalities determined by that service.
The judge may furthermore subject the convicted person to particular individualised conditions in the interests of the victims. These conditions relate to the prohibition on frequenting certain places or contacting the victim and/or on compensating the latter.]1

(1)	<Inserted by L 2014-02-07/15, art. 7, 110; In force: undetermined and at the latest on 01-05-2016 (see L 2014-05-08/55, art. 6, itself modified by L 2015-11-23/02, art. 13), art. 7 rewritten by L 2016-02-05/11, art. 44>
(2)	<L 2018-07-11/02, art. 7, 131; In force: 28-07-2018>

Art. 37quater. [

###### §

1er. As soon as the conviction to an electronic monitoring sentence becomes final, the clerk informs the competent service for electronic monitoring with a view to having this sentence executed. To this end, that service contacts the convicted person within seven working days following the information, determines the concrete modalities of execution of the sentence, after having heard the convicted person and taking into account his observations, and notifies the competent public prosecutor without delay.

###### § 2

Without prejudice to the application of Article 20 of the Act of 5 August 1992 on the police function, the public prosecutor is responsible for the supervision of the convicted person. The officials of the competent service for electronic monitoring monitor the execution of the electronic monitoring sentence and ensure the follow-up or guidance of the convicted person.

###### § 3

If the electronic monitoring sentence is not executed in whole or in part in accordance with the provisions of Article 37ter, § 5, the official of the competent service for electronic monitoring informs the competent public prosecutor without delay. The latter may then decide to proceed with the execution of the imprisonment sentence fixed in the judicial decision, taking into account the part of the electronic monitoring sentence which has already been executed by the convicted person. In this case, one day of electronic monitoring sentence executed is equivalent to one day of imprisonment. If the total or partial non-execution concerns new offences, it must be established by a decision that has become final that the convicted person has committed an offence or

a crime, or an equivalent offence taken into account in accordance with Article 99bis, during the execution of the electronic monitoring sentence.
The competent public prosecutor gives reasons for his decision and communicates it by means of the fastest written means of communication:
-	to the convicted person;
-	to the chief of the local police of the municipality where the convicted person resides;
-	to the national database referred to in Article 44/2 of the Act of 5 August 1992 on the police function;
-	to the competent service for electronic monitoring.

###### § 4

If the sentence of electronic monitoring reaches or exceeds three months, the convicted person may request a suspension of the control carried out by electronic means after having served one third of the duration of the sentence. He is informed of this possibility by the service competent for electronic monitoring as soon as the sentence is put into execution. As soon as he meets the time conditions, the convicted person may address to the competent public prosecutor's office a written request with a view to obtaining this suspension. The convicted person sends a copy of this written request to the service competent for electronic monitoring.
Within fifteen days, the service competent for electronic monitoring renders an opinion to the competent public prosecutor's office concerning compliance with the programme of the concrete content of the electronic monitoring and, where applicable, the individualised special conditions imposed on the convicted person. This opinion indicates whether the convicted person has committed new offences during the execution of the sentence of electronic monitoring. In addition, it includes a reasoned proposal for granting or rejecting the suspension of the control carried out by electronic means and sets out, where applicable, the special conditions that the service competent for electronic monitoring considers necessary to impose on the convicted person.
The competent public prosecutor's office grants within one month after receipt of the opinion the suspension of the control carried out by electronic means in the case where the convicted person has not committed new offences and he has complied with the programme of the concrete content of the electronic monitoring and, where applicable, the individualised special conditions that had been imposed on him.
When the suspension of the control carried out by electronic means is granted, the convicted person is subject to a probationary period for the part of the sentence of electronic monitoring that he still has to serve. In this case, one day of the probationary period is equivalent to one day of the sentence of electronic monitoring imposed. The convicted person is subject to the general conditions, as well as, where applicable, to the

special conditions that have been imposed on him.
The competent public prosecutor's office communicates its decision by the fastest written means of communication:
-	to the convicted person;
-	to the chief of the local police force of the municipality where the convicted person resides;
-	to the national database referred to in Article 44/2 of the Act of 5 August 1992 on the police function;
-	to the service competent for electronic monitoring.
In case of rejection of a request for suspension, a new request may only be submitted after the expiry of a period of two months from the rejection.
In case of non-compliance with the general conditions and, where applicable, the special conditions imposed on the convicted person, the suspension of the control carried out by electronic means may be revoked.
The competent public prosecutor's office hears the convicted person in his observations on the matter. If the convicted person does not respond to the summons for the purpose of being heard, this public prosecutor's office may decide to revoke the suspension of the control carried out by electronic means or to proceed with the execution of the subsidiary imprisonment. If the non-compliance concerns the general condition prohibiting the commission of new offences, it must be established by a decision that has acquired the force of res judicata that the convicted person has committed a misdemeanour or a felony, or an equivalent offence taken into account in accordance with Article 99bis, during the execution of the sentence of electronic monitoring or during the suspension of the control carried out by electronic means.
The decision to revoke the suspension of the control carried out by electronic means includes a decision on:
-	the special conditions linked to the suspension, imposed by the public prosecutor's office;
-	the execution of the electronic monitoring for the remaining duration of the probationary period;
-	the reinstatement of the special conditions imposed where applicable by the trial court.
The competent public prosecutor's office communicates its decision by means of the fastest written means of communication:
-	to the convicted person;
-	to the chief of the local police force of the municipality where the convicted person resides;
-	to the national database referred to in Article 44/2 of the Act of 5 August 1992

on the police function;
-	to the service competent for electronic monitoring.

###### § 5

The public prosecutor's office referred to in paragraphs 1 to 4 is the public prosecutor's office before the trial court that pronounced the conviction to a sentence under electronic monitoring.]1

(1)<Inserted by L 2014-02-07/15, art. 8, 110; In force: undetermined and at the latest on 01-05-2016 (see L 2014-05-08/55, art. 6, itself modified by L 2015-11-23/02, art. 13), art. 8, rewritten by L 2016-02-05/11, art. 45>

SECTION Vter. - ([1 former section Vbis renumbered as new section Vter]1) ON THE SENTENCE OF COMMUNITY SERVICE <Inserted by L 2002-04-17/33, art. 3; In force
: 07-05-2002>

(1)	<L 2014-02-07/15, art. 9, 110; In force: undetermined and at the latest on 01-05-2016>

Art. 37quinquies.([1 former art. 37ter renumbered as new art. 37quinquies]1)
<Inserted by L 2002-04-17/33, art. 3; In force: 07-05-2002> § 1er. When an act is of a nature to entail a police penalty or a correctional penalty, the judge may sentence as a principal penalty to a sentence of community service. The judge provides, within the limits of the penalties provided for the offence and by the law according to his jurisdiction, a sentence of imprisonment or a fine that may be applicable in case of non-execution of the sentence of community service.
[3 The sentence of community service may not be pronounced for acts:
1° which would be punishable, if they were not transmuted into misdemeanours, by a maximum penalty exceeding twenty years of imprisonment;
2° referred to in Articles 375 to 377;
3° referred to in Articles 379 to 387, if the acts were committed on minors or with the help of minors;
4° referred to in Articles 393 to 397.]

###### § 

2. The duration of a sentence of community service may not be less than twenty hours nor more than three hundred hours. A sentence of community service equal to or less than forty-five hours constitutes a police penalty. A sentence of community service of more than forty-five hours constitutes a correctional penalty.
The sentence of community service must be executed within twelve months following the date on

which the judicial decision has acquired the force of res judicata. The probation commission may ex officio or at the request of the convicted person extend this period.

###### § 3

When a sentence of community service is envisaged by the judge, requested by the public prosecutor's office or sought by the accused, the judge informs the latter, before the close of the proceedings, of the scope of such a sentence and hears him in his observations. The judge may also take into account, in this regard, the interests of any victims. The judge may only pronounce the sentence of community service if the accused is present or represented at the hearing and after he has given, either in person or through his counsel, his consent.
[2 The judge who refuses to pronounce a sentence of community service requested by the public prosecutor's office or sought by the accused, must give reasons for his decision.]

###### §

4. The judge determines the duration of the community service sentence and may give indications concerning the concrete content of the community service sentence.

(1)	<L 2014-02-07/15, art. 9, 110; En vigueur : indéterminée et au plus tard au 01-05-2016>
(2)	<L 2014-04-10/80, art. 4, 111; En vigueur : indéterminée et au plus tard le 01-05-2016 (voir L 2014-05-08/55, art. 6, modifié lui-même par L 2015-11-23/02, art. 13)>
(3)	<L 2016-02-05/11, art. 12, 114; En vigueur : 29-02-2016>

Art. 37sexies.([1 ancien art. 37quater renumérotée en nouvel art. 37sexies]1)
<Inséré par L 2002-04-17/33, art. 3; En vigueur : 07-05-2002> § 1er. The community service sentence is performed gratuitously by the convicted person during the time left free by his or her possible educational or professional activities.
The community service sentence may only be performed with the public services of the State, the municipalities, the provinces, the communities and the regions or with non-profit associations or foundations with a social, scientific or cultural purpose.
The community service sentence may not consist of work which, in the designated public service or association, is generally performed by remunerated workers.

###### § 2

With a view to the application of Article 37ter, the public prosecutor's office, the investigating judge, the courts of instruction and the trial courts may instruct the section of the Service of the Houses of Justice of the (FPS Justice) of the judicial district of the place of residence of the accused, the defendant or the convicted person to draw up a brief information report and/or a

social inquiry. <L 2006-12-27/33, art. 35, 1°, 061; En vigueur : 07-01-2007> (The King specifies the rules relating to the brief information report and the social inquiry.
These reports and these inquiries may contain only the relevant elements of a nature to inform the authority which addressed the request to the service of the houses of justice on the appropriateness of the measure or sentence envisaged.) <L 2006-12-27/33, art. 35, 2°, 061; En vigueur : 07-01-2007>

###### § 3

(Each district section of the Service of the Houses of Justice of the FPS Justice establishes twice a year a report of the existing activities which lend themselves to the performance of the community service sentence.) The section delivers a copy of this report to the president of the court of first instance and to the public prosecutor of the district concerned and, upon simple request, to any person able to justify an interest. <L 2006-12-27/33, art. 35, 3°, 061; En vigueur : 07-01-2007> (§ 4. At the federal and local levels, consultation structures relating to the application of the [2 community service sentence and the autonomous probation sentence]2 are created. These consultation structures have the mission of bringing together on a regular basis the bodies concerned with the execution of the [2 community service sentence and the autonomous probation sentence]2 in order to evaluate their collaboration. The King determines the modalities of composition and operation of these consultation structures.) <L 2006-12-27/33, art. 35, 4°, 061; En vigueur : 07-01-2007>

(1)	<L 2014-02-07/15, art. 9, 110; En vigueur : indéterminée et au plus tard au 01-05-2016>
(2)	<L 2014-04-10/80, art. 5, 111; En vigueur : indéterminée et au plus tard le 01-05-2016 (voir L 2014-05-08/55, art. 6, modifié lui-même par L 2015-11-23/02, art. 13)>

Art. 37septies.([1 ancien art. 37quinquies renumérotée en nouvel art. 37septies)]1) <Inséré par L 2002-04-17/33, art. 3; En vigueur : 07-05-2002> § 1er. The convicted person to whom a community service sentence has been imposed pursuant to Article 37ter is supervised by a justice assistant of the Service of the Houses of Justice of the (FPS Justice) of the judicial district of the place of residence of the convicted person. <L 2006-12-27/33, art. 36, 1°, 061; En vigueur : 07-01-2007>
The execution of the community service sentence is monitored by the probation commission of the place of residence of the convicted person to which the justice assistant reports.

###### § 2

When the judicial decision pronouncing a community service sentence has become

res judicata, the clerk transmits within twenty-four hours a copy thereof to the president of the competent probation commission as well as to (the competent district section of the Service of the Houses of Justice of the FPS Justice), which designates without delay the justice assistant referred to in § 1er. [2 ...]2
(The territorial jurisdiction of the probation commission is determined by the place of residence of the convicted person at the time when the judgment or the order becomes res judicata. When the person concerned resides outside the territory of the Kingdom, the territorially competent probation commission is that of the place where the conviction was pronounced at first instance.
When, in exceptional cases, the commission considers it appropriate, for a person convicted to a community service sentence who makes a reasoned request to that effect, to transfer jurisdiction to the commission of the place of his or her new residence, it takes a reasoned decision, after that other commission has given a conforming opinion within a period of two months. For a person without residence in the Kingdom, jurisdiction may be transferred according to the same procedure to another probation commission, without it being required in that case that it be the commission of the place of his or her new residence.) <L 2006-12-27/33, art. 36, 4°, 061; En vigueur : 07-01-2007>

###### § 3

After having heard the convicted person and taken account of his or her observations, the justice assistant determines the concrete content of the sentence, in compliance with the indications referred to in Article 37ter, § 4, under the supervision of the probation commission which, of its own motion, upon requisition of the public prosecutor's office or at the request of the convicted person, may at any time, and also in compliance with the indications referred to in Article 37ter, § 4, specify and adapt it.
(The concrete content of the community service sentence is notified in an agreement to be signed by the convicted person, of which the justice assistant gives him or her a copy. The justice assistant also communicates a copy of the signed agreement to the probation commission, within a period of three working days.) <L 2006-12-27/33, art. 36, 5°, 061; En vigueur : 07-01-2007>

###### § 4

In the event of total or partial non-execution of the community service sentence, the justice assistant informs the probation commission without delay. The commission summons the convicted person by registered mail [2 or by an electronic means to be defined by the King]2 more than ten days before the date fixed for the examination of the case and informs his or her counsel. The file of the commission is made available for five days to the convicted person and his or her counsel.
The commission, sitting in the absence of the public prosecutor's office, draws up a report

[2 with reasons]2 with a view to the application of the substitute sentence.
(The report is sent by ordinary letter to the convicted person, to the public prosecutor's office.) and to the justice assistant.) < L 2006-12-27/33, art. 36, 6°, 061; En vigueur : 07-01-2007>
In this case, the public prosecutor's office may decide to execute the sentence of imprisonment or the fine provided for in the judicial decision, taking into account the community service sentence which has already been executed by the convicted person.

(1)	<L 2014-02-07/15, art. 9, 110; En vigueur : indéterminée et au plus tard au 01-05-2016>
(2)	<L 2014-04-10/80, art. 6, 111; En vigueur : indéterminée et au plus tard le 01-05-2016 (voir L 2014-05-08/55, art. 6, modifié lui-même par L 2015-11-23/02, art. 13)>

SECTION Vquater. [1 - Of the autonomous probation sentence]1

(1)	<Inséré par L 2014-04-10/80, art. 7, 111; En vigueur : indéterminée et au plus tard le 01-05-2016 (voir L 2014-05-08/55, art. 6, modifié lui-même par L 2015-11-23/02, art. 13)>

Art. 37octies.[

###### §

1er. Where an act is such as to give rise to a police penalty or a correctional penalty, the judge may impose, as a principal penalty, an autonomous probation sentence.
An autonomous probation sentence consists of the obligation to comply with specific conditions during a fixed period, determined by the judge in accordance with § 2.
The judge shall provide, within the limits of the penalties prescribed for the offence and by law according to the court's jurisdiction, an imprisonment sentence or a fine which may be applicable in the event of non-execution of the autonomous probation sentence.
The autonomous probation sentence may not be imposed for acts:
1° which would be punishable, if they were not transmuted into offences, by a maximum penalty exceeding twenty years' imprisonment;
2° referred to in articles 375 to 377;
3° referred to in articles 379 to 387, if the acts were committed against minors or with the assistance of minors;
4° referred to in articles 393 to 397.

###### § 2

The duration of the autonomous probation sentence may not be less than six months nor more than two years. An autonomous probation sentence of twelve months or less than twelve months constitutes a police penalty. An autonomous probation sentence of one year or more than one year constitutes a correctional penalty.

###### § 3

Where an autonomous probation sentence is being considered by the judge, requested by the public prosecutor or sought by the accused, the judge shall inform the accused, before the close of the proceedings, of the scope of such a sentence and hear him in his observations. The judge may also take into account, in this regard, the interests of any victims. The judge may only impose the autonomous probation sentence if the accused is present or represented at the hearing and after he has given his consent, either in person or through his counsel.
The judge who refuses to impose an autonomous probation sentence requested by the public prosecutor or sought by the accused must give reasons for his decision.

###### § 4

The judge determines the duration of the autonomous probation sentence and gives indications concerning the content of the autonomous probation sentence.

###### § 5

At federal and local levels, the consultation structures relating to the application of the work penalty and the autonomous probation sentence operate in accordance with the provisions of article 37sexies, § 4.]1

(1)	<Inséré par L 2014-04-10/80, art. 8, 111; En vigueur : indéterminée et au plus tard le 01-05-2016 (voir L 2014-05-08/55, art. 6, modifié lui-même par L 2015-11-23/02, art. 13), art. 8 modifié par L 2016-02-05/11, art. 51, 114>

Art. 37novies.[

###### §

1er. Anyone who has been sentenced to an autonomous probation sentence in accordance with article 37octies shall be subject to judicial guidance exercised by a justice assistant of the Service of Houses of Justice of the Federal Public Service Justice of the judicial district of the place of his residence.
The execution of the autonomous probation sentence is monitored by the probation commission of the place of residence of the convicted person to which the justice assistant reports.
When the judicial decision imposing the autonomous probation sentence has become final, the clerk shall transmit within twenty-four hours a copy thereof to the president of the competent probation commission as well as to the competent district section of the Service of Houses of Justice of the Federal Public Service Justice, which shall appoint the justice assistant without delay.

Within one month following the appointment of the justice assistant, and thereafter whenever he deems it useful or whenever the commission so requests, and at least every six months, he shall report to the probation commission on compliance with the conditions. He shall propose, where appropriate, such measures as he deems useful.

###### § 2

The territorial jurisdiction of the probation commission is determined by the place of residence of the convicted person at the time when the judgment or decision becomes final. Where the person concerned resides outside the territory of the Kingdom, the territorially competent probation commission is that of the place where the conviction was imposed at first instance.
Where, in exceptional cases, the commission considers it appropriate, for a person sentenced to an autonomous probation sentence who makes a reasoned request to that effect, to transfer jurisdiction to the probation commission of the place of his new residence, it shall take a reasoned decision, after that other commission has given a conforming opinion within a period of two months. For a person without residence in the Kingdom, jurisdiction may be transferred according to the same procedure to another probation commission, without it being required in that case that it be the commission of the place of his new residence.

###### § 3

The probation commission determines the specific content of the autonomous probation sentence, on the basis of the report of the justice assistant who has heard the convicted person and in compliance with the indications referred to in article 37octies, § 4.
The specific content of the autonomous probation sentence is notified in an agreement to be signed by the convicted person, of which the justice assistant gives him a copy. The justice assistant also communicates a copy of the signed agreement to the probation commission, within a period of three working days.]1

(1)	<Inséré par L 2014-04-10/80, art. 9, 111; En vigueur : indéterminée et au plus tard le 01-05-2016 (voir L 2014-05-08/55, art. 6, modifié lui-même par L 2015-11-23/02, art. 13)>

Art. 37decies.[

###### §

1er. The probation commission may suspend in whole or in part the specific content of the autonomous probation sentence, specify it or adapt it to the circumstances, either of its own motion, or at the request of the public prosecutor, or at the request of the convicted person. In the case where one of the conditions of the autonomous probation sentence could not be fulfilled during the initial probation period without this being due to the will of the convicted person, the probation commission may extend the probation period once by a maximum of one year so that the convicted person may satisfy the condition.

If the probation commission considers it necessary to take one of the measures referred to in paragraph 1, the president shall summon the person concerned, by registered mail or by an electronic means to be defined by the King, more than ten days before the date fixed for the examination of the case. The file of the commission shall be made available for ten days to the person concerned and to his counsel, if any.
If the probation commission considers that the autonomous probation sentence has been executed, it may decide that it shall end, even if the period fixed by the judge has not yet expired.
The decision of the probation commission referred to in paragraph 1 or paragraph 3 shall be reasoned. That decision shall be notified to the person concerned and to the public prosecutor. Notification shall be made to the public prosecutor by ordinary letter and to the person concerned by registered mail or by an electronic means to be defined by the King, within three days, not including Saturdays, Sundays and public holidays.

###### § 2

The public prosecutor and the person sentenced to the autonomous probation penalty may, the former by requisition and the latter by application, bring before the court of first instance at which the commission is established, an appeal against decisions made by the commission pursuant to § 1 or pursuant to Article 37novies, § 3.
The requisition and the application must be written and reasoned. The appeal must be brought within ten days of notification of the commission's decision. It has suspensive effect, unless the commission decides otherwise.
The president of the court called upon to rule shall have indicated more than ten days in advance, in a special register kept at the registry, the place, day and hour of appearance. The registrar gives notice thereof to the person sentenced to the autonomous probation penalty by registered mail or by electronic means to be determined by the King at least ten days before the appearance. During this period, the file is deposited at the registry and made available to the convicted person and his or her counsel if any. The court sits and rules in chambers.
If the court allows the appeal, it may reverse the commission's decision. The decision rendered on this appeal is subject neither to appeal nor to opposition.]1

(1)	<Inséré par L 2014-04-10/80, art. 10, 111; En vigueur : indéterminée et au plus tard le 01-05-2016 (voir L 2014-05-08/55, art. 6, modifié lui-même par L 2015-11-

23/02, art. 13)>>

Art. 37undecies.[1 In the event of total or partial non-execution of the autonomous probation penalty, the probation officer informs the probation commission without delay. The commission summons the convicted person by registered mail or by electronic means to be determined by the King more than ten days before the date set for examination of the case and informs his or her counsel. The commission's file is made available to the convicted person and his or her counsel if any for five days.
The commission, sitting in the absence of the public prosecutor, draws up a reasoned report with a view to applying the substitute penalty.
The report is sent by ordinary letter to the convicted person, the public prosecutor and the probation officer.
In this case, the public prosecutor may decide to execute the custodial sentence or the fine provided for in the judicial decision, taking into account the autonomous probation penalty that has already been executed by the convicted person.]1

(1)	<Inséré par L 2014-04-10/80, art. 11, 111; En vigueur : indéterminée et au plus tard le 01-05-2016 (voir L 2014-05-08/55, art. 6, modifié lui-même par L 2015-11-23/02, art. 13)>>

##### SECTION VI. - PENALTIES COMMON TO THE THREE TYPES OF OFFENCE

###### Sous-section I. - (Fines applicable to natural persons)

<Inséré par L 1999-05-04/60, art. 7; En vigueur : 02-07-1999>

###### Art. 38

The fine for a petty offence is at least one [euro] and at most twenty-five [euros], except in cases excepted by law. <L 2000-06-26/42, art. 2, En vigueur : 01-01-2002>
The fine for a felony or misdemeanour is at least twenty-six [euros] <L 2000-06-26/42, art. 2, En vigueur : 01-01-2002>.
Fines shall be collected for the benefit of the State.

###### Art. 39

The fine is imposed individually on each of the persons convicted in respect of the same offence.

###### Art. 40

In default of payment within two months from the judgment or order, if it is adversarial, or from its service, if it is by default, the fine may be replaced by imprisonment the duration of which shall be fixed by the judgment or order of conviction, and which shall not exceed six months for persons convicted in respect of a felony, three months for persons convicted in respect of a misdemeanour, and three days for persons convicted in respect of a petty offence.
Convicted persons subject to subsidiary imprisonment may be detained in the institution where they served the principal penalty.
If only a fine has been imposed, the imprisonment to be served in default of payment is treated as correctional or police imprisonment, according to the nature of the conviction.

###### Art. 41

In all cases, the convicted person may be released from this imprisonment by paying the fine; he or she may not avoid proceedings against his or her property by offering to serve the imprisonment.

###### Sous-section II. - (Fines applicable to legal persons)

<Inséré par L 1999-05-04/60, art. 8; En vigueur : 02-07-1999>

Art. 41bis. <Inséré par L 1999-05-04/60, art. 8; En vigueur : 02-07-1999> § 1er. The fines applicable to offences committed by legal persons are:
in criminal and correctional matters:
-	where the law provides for the act a custodial sentence for life: a fine of two hundred and forty thousand [euros] to seven hundred and twenty thousand [euros] <L 2000-06-26/42, art. 2, En vigueur : 01-01-2002>;
-	where the law provides for the act a custodial sentence and a fine, or only one of these penalties: a minimum fine of five hundred [euros] multiplied by the number of months corresponding to the minimum custodial sentence, and not being less than the minimum fine provided for the act; the maximum amounts to two thousand [euros] multiplied by the number of months corresponding to the maximum custodial sentence, and not being less than double the maximum fine provided for the act <L 2000-06-26/42, art. 2, En vigueur : 01-01-2002>;
-	where the law provides for the act only a fine: the minimum and the

maximum are those provided by law for the act; in police matters:
-	a fine of twenty-five [euros] to two hundred and fifty [euros] <L 2000-06-26/42, art. 2, En vigueur : 01-01-2002>.

###### § 2

For the determination of the penalty provided for in § 1er, the provisions of Book I are applicable.

###### Sous-section III. - (special confiscation)

<Inséré par L 1999-05-04/60, art. 9; En vigueur : 02-07-1999>

###### Art. 42. Special confiscation applies to

1° Things forming the object of the offence and those which have been used or which have been intended to commit it, when ownership thereof belongs to the convicted person; 2° Things which have been produced by the offence.
(3° Patrimonial advantages derived directly from the offence, property and assets which have been substituted for them and the income from these advantages invested.) <L 1990-07-17/30, art. 1, 004; En vigueur : 25-08-1990>

###### Art. 43

Special confiscation (applying to the things referred to in 1° and 2° of Article 42) shall always be pronounced for a crime or offence. [1 The confiscation of things which have served or which were intended to commit the crime or offence shall be ordered, except where it has the effect of subjecting the convicted person to an unreasonably heavy penalty.]1 <L 1990-07-17/30, art. 2, 004; En vigueur : 25-08-1990>
It shall be pronounced for a petty offence only in the cases determined by law.

(1)	<L 2018-03-18/14, art. 19, 129; En vigueur : 12-05-2018>

Art. 43bis.<Inséré par L 1990-07-17/30, art. 3, 004; En vigueur : 25-08-1990> (Special confiscation applying to the things referred to in Article 42, 3°, may always be pronounced by the judge, but only to the extent that it is required in writing by the public prosecutor.) <L 2002-12-19/86, art. 1, 039; En vigueur : 24-02-2003>
[3 If the things provided for in paragraph 1 and the things which have served or which were intended to commit the offence cannot]3 be found in the patrimony

of the convicted person, the judge shall proceed to their monetary valuation and the confiscation shall relate to a sum of money equivalent to them.
Where the confiscated things belong to the civil party, they shall be returned to it. The confiscated things shall likewise be attributed to it where the judge has pronounced their confiscation on the ground that they constitute goods or values substituted by the convicted person for things belonging to the civil party or because they constitute the equivalent of such things within the meaning of paragraph 2 of this article.
Any other third party claiming a right over the confiscated thing may assert that right within a period and according to procedures determined by the King.
[1 Special confiscation of immovable property must or may be pronounced by the judge, according to the applicable legal basis, but only to the extent that it has been required in writing by the public prosecutor.
The written requisition of the public prosecutor seeking the confiscation of immovable property which has not been criminally seized in accordance with the applicable formalities is, on pain of inadmissibility, registered free of charge in the margin of the last transcribed title or of the judgment referred to in Article 1, paragraphs 1 and 2, of the Mortgage Act of 16 December 1851. The public prosecutor attaches proof of the marginal notation to the criminal file before the close of the proceedings. The public prosecutor requests, where appropriate, the free cancellation of the marginal notation.]1
[2 The judge reduces as necessary the amount of the patrimonial advantages referred to in Article 42, 3°, or of the monetary valuation referred to in paragraph 2 in order not to subject the convicted person to an unreasonably heavy penalty.]2

Art. 43ter. <Inséré par L 1997-05-20/50, art. 12, En vigueur : 13-07-1997> Special confiscation applying to the things referred to (in Articles 42, 43bis and 43quater) may also be pronounced where those things are located outside the territory of Belgium. <L 2002-12-19/86, art. 3, 039; En vigueur : 24-02-2003>

Art. 43quater.<inséré par L 2002-12-19/86, art. 4; En vigueur : 24-02-2003> § 1er. [2 Without prejudice to Article 43bis, paragraphs 3 and 4, the patrimonial advantages

referred to in paragraph 2, the goods and values which have been substituted for them and the income derived from the invested advantages found in the patrimony or in the possession of a person may, at the request of the public prosecutor, be confiscated or that person may be sentenced to pay an amount which the judge considers to correspond to the value of those things if he or she has been found guilty:
1° either of one or more offences referred to:
a)	in Articles 136sexies and 136septies, 1°;
b)	in Article 137, provided that those offences are punishable by one of the penalties provided for in Article 138, § 1er, 4° to 10°, and that they are of a nature to generate patrimonial advantages, in Article 140, provided that that crime or offence is of a nature to generate patrimonial advantages, in Articles 140bis to 140sexies, provided that those offences are of a nature to generate patrimonial advantages, in Article 140septies, provided that that offence is punishable by one of the penalties provided for in Article 140septies, § 1er, third and fourth indent, and that it is of a nature to generate patrimonial advantages, and in Article 141;
c)	in Articles 162, 163, 173, 180 and 186;

g)	in Articles 504bis and 504ter;

i)	in Article 2bis, § 1er, of the Act of 24 February 1921 concerning the traffic in poisonous, soporific, narcotic, psychotropic, disinfectant or antiseptic substances and substances which may be used for the illicit manufacture of narcotic and psychotropic substances, provided that the acts relate to the importation, exportation, manufacture, sale or offering for sale of the substances referred to in that article, or in Article 2bis, § 3, b), or in § 4, b);
j)	in Article 2quater, 4°, of the same Act;
k)	in Articles 77bis to 77quinquies of the Act of 15 December 1980 on access to the territory, residence, establishment and removal of aliens;
l)	in Article 10, § 1er, 2°, of the Act of 15 July 1985 on the use of substances having a hormonal effect, an anti-hormonal effect, a beta-adrenergic effect or a production-stimulating effect in animals;
2° either of the offences referred to in Article 324ter;

3° either one or more offences referred to below, where they have been committed within the framework of a criminal organisation, as defined in Article 324bis:
a)	in Articles 468, 469, 470, 471 or 472;
b)	in Article 475;
c)	in Articles 477 to 477sexies or 488bis;
d)	in Article 8 of the Act of 5 August 1991 on the importation, exportation, transit and the fight against the traffic in arms, ammunition and equipment to be used specifically for military or law enforcement purposes and the related technology;
e)	in Articles 1er and 8 of the Royal Decree of 12 April 1974 on certain operations concerning substances with hormonal, anti-hormonal, anabolic, beta-adrenergic, anti-infectious, antiparasitic and anti-inflammatory action, for offences punished in accordance with the Act of 24 February 1921 concerning the traffic in poisonous, soporific, narcotic, psychotropic, disinfectant or antiseptic substances and substances which may be used for the illicit manufacture of narcotic and psychotropic substances;
4° either of several offences prosecuted collectively and whose gravity, purpose and mutual relationship enable the court to decide certainly and necessarily that those acts were committed within the framework of serious tax fraud, whether organised or not.]

###### §

2. The confiscation referred to in § 1 may be pronounced against the perpetrators, co-perpetrators and accomplices convicted of one or more of the offences listed in this article and under the conditions defined in § 1 if the convicted person has acquired additional patrimonial advantages during a relevant period while there are serious and concrete indications that these derive from the offence for which he was convicted or [2 from offences likely to give rise, directly or indirectly, to an economic advantage provided that they fall under the same heading, provided for in paragraph 1, as the offence which is the subject of the conviction]2 and that the convicted person has not been able to make the contrary plausible.
This contrary may also be made plausible by any third party claiming to have a right to these advantages.

###### § 3

The period considered relevant within the meaning of this article is the period beginning five years before the indictment of the person and running until the date of pronouncement.

The serious and concrete indications referred to in § 2 may be drawn from all credible elements which have been submitted to the court in a regular manner and which show an imbalance of any interest between, on the one hand, the temporary or constant increase in the assets and expenditure of the convicted person during the relevant period, of which the public prosecutor provides proof, and, on the other hand, the temporary or constant increase in the assets and expenditure of the convicted person during that period for which he can make it plausible that they do not derive from the facts for which he was convicted or from identical facts. [2 ...]2
When the court orders special confiscation within the meaning of this article, it may decide not to take into account part of the relevant period or income, property and values which it determines if it considers such a measure appropriate in order not to subject the convicted person to an unreasonably heavy penalty.

###### § 4

The assets at the disposal of a criminal organisation must be confiscated, subject to the rights of third parties in good faith.

(1)<L 2013-07-15/02, art. 14, 094; En vigueur : 29-07-2013>
(2)<L 2018-03-18/14, art. 21, 129; En vigueur : 12-05-2018>

#### CHAPITRE III

OTHER CONVICTIONS WHICH MAY BE PRONOUNCED FOR CRIMES, OFFENCES OR CONTRAVENTIONS.

###### Art. 44

The conviction to the penalties established by law shall always be pronounced without prejudice to restitutions and damages which may be due to the parties.

###### Art. 45

When the law has not regulated the damages, the court or tribunal shall determine the amount thereof, without however being able to order the application thereof to any work whatsoever, even with the consent of the injured party.

###### Art. 46

[1 The court or tribunal which finds guilty of one of the offences referred to in articles 375, 398 to 400, 402, 403, 405, 409, §§ 1 to 3 and 5, and 422bis, a person likely to be called as legal heir to the succession of the victim, may also pronounce the unworthiness to inherit of the perpetrator, co-perpetrator or accomplice, who shall therefore be excluded from the succession of the victim.]1

(1)<rétabli par L 2012-12-10/14, art. 42, 088; En vigueur : 21-01-2013>

###### Art. 47. (Abrogé)

<L 31-01-1980, art. 4, 1°>

###### Art. 48. (Abrogé)

<L 31-01-1980, art. 4, 1°>

###### Art. 49

When the property of the convicted person is insufficient to cover the convictions to the fine, restitutions and damages, the last two convictions shall have preference.
In case of competition of the fine with the costs of justice due to the State, payments made by the convicted persons shall be imputed first to these costs. (These payments interrupt the limitation period for both the fine and the costs of justice.) <L 2006-12-27/32, art. 302, 062; En vigueur : 07-01-2007>

###### Art. 50

All individuals convicted of the same offence are jointly and severally liable for restitutions and damages.
They are jointly and severally liable for costs when they have been convicted by the same judgment or decision.
Nevertheless, the judge may exempt all or some of the convicted persons from joint and several liability, by indicating the reasons for this exemption, and by determining the proportion of costs to be borne individually by each of them.
Individuals convicted by separate judgments or decisions are jointly and severally liable for costs only in respect of acts of prosecution which were common to them.

Art. 50bis. <Inséré par L 1999-05-04/60, art. 10; En vigueur : 02-07-1999> No one may be held civilly liable for payment of a fine to which another person is convicted, if he is convicted for the same facts.

#### CHAPITRE IV. - ATTEMPT TO COMMIT A CRIME OR OFFENCE

###### Art. 51

There is a punishable attempt when the resolution to commit a crime or offence has been manifested by external acts which form a commencement of execution of that crime or offence, and which have been suspended or have failed to have their effect only by circumstances independent

of the will of the perpetrator.

###### Art. 52

The attempt to commit a crime is punished by the penalty immediately below that of the crime itself, in accordance with articles 80 and 81.
[1 Attempts to commit crimes punishable by imprisonment for life or detention for life shall however be punished respectively by imprisonment of twenty years to thirty years or by detention of twenty years to thirty years.]1

(1)<L 2016-02-05/11, art. 13, 114; En vigueur : 29-02-2016>

###### Art. 53

The law determines in which cases and by which penalties attempts to commit offences are punished.

#### CHAPITRE V. - RECIDIVISM

###### Art. 54. (Voir NOTE sous TITRE)

<L 2003-01-23/42, art. 12, 040; En vigueur : 13-03-2003> Whoever, having been convicted of a criminal penalty, commits a crime carrying imprisonment of five years to ten years, may be convicted to imprisonment of ten years to fifteen years.
If the crime carries imprisonment of ten years to fifteen years, the offender may be convicted to imprisonment of fifteen years to twenty years.
He shall be convicted to at least seventeen years of this penalty if the crime carries imprisonment of fifteen years to twenty years.

###### Art. 55. (Voir NOTE sous TITRE)

Whoever, having been convicted of a criminal penalty, commits a crime punished by detention of five years to ten years, may be convicted to detention of ten years to fifteen years.
If the crime is punished by detention of ten years to fifteen years, the offender may be convicted to (detention of fifteen years to twenty years). <L 2003-01-23/42, art. 13, 040; En vigueur : 13-03-2003>
He shall be convicted to at least seventeen years of detention, if the crime carries (detention of fifteen years to twenty years). <L 2003-01-23/42, art. 13, 040; En vigueur
: 13-03-2003>

###### Art. 56

Whoever, after a conviction to a criminal penalty, commits an offence, may be convicted to a penalty double the maximum provided

by law for the offence.
The same penalty may be pronounced in case of prior conviction to imprisonment of at least one year, if the convicted person committed the new offence before the expiry of five years since he served or prescribed his penalty.
[1 Even in the cases referred to in paragraphs 1 and 2, if the new offence is a crime which has been correctionalised or for which the court of assizes has admitted the existence of mitigating circumstances, the duration of the penalty of imprisonment may not exceed that of the maximum penalty of imprisonment provided by law for that crime or forty years if the said penalty is imprisonment for life.]1
[1 In no case may the penalty pronounced exceed one year of electronic monitoring penalty, three hundred hours of work penalty or two years of autonomous probation penalty.]1

(1)<L 2016-02-05/11, art. 14, 114; En vigueur : 29-02-2016>

###### Art. 57

The rules established for recidivism shall be applied, in accordance with the preceding articles, in case of a prior conviction pronounced by a military tribunal, for an act qualified as a crime or offence by the ordinary criminal laws, and to a penalty provided for by those same laws.
If, for that act, a penalty provided for by military laws has been pronounced, the courts and tribunals, in assessing recidivism, shall have regard only to the minimum of the penalty that the act punished by the first judgment could have entailed under the ordinary criminal laws.

Art. 57bis. [1 The rules established for recidivism, provided for in articles 54 to 56, are applied in case of a prior conviction taken into account in accordance with article 99bis.]1

(1)<Inserted by L 2014-04-25/23, art. 60, 104; In force: 24-05-2014>

#### CHAPTER VI. - CONCURRENCE OF SEVERAL OFFENCES

###### Art. 58

Any individual convicted of several petty offences shall incur the penalty for each of them.
(Where work penalties are pronounced, the duration thereof may be cumulated up to a maximum of three hundred hours.) <L 2002-04-17/33, art. 4, 035; In

force: 07-05-2002>
[1 [2 Where electronic monitoring penalties are pronounced, their duration may not exceed one year.]2 ]1
[2 Where autonomous probation penalties are pronounced, their duration may not exceed two years.]2

(1)	<L 2014-02-07/15, art. 10, 110; In force: indeterminate and at the latest 01-05-2016, repealed on 29-02-2014, before its entry into force by L 2016-06-05/11, art. 42>
(2)	<L 2014-04-10/80, art. 12, 111; In force: indeterminate and at the latest 01-05-2016 (see L 2014-05-08/55, art. 6, itself modified by L 2015-11-23/02, art. 13), art. 12 modified by L 2016-02-05/11, art. 52, 114>

###### Art. 59

In case of concurrence of one or more offences with one or more petty offences, [1 all fines, autonomous probation penalties, work penalties, electronic monitoring penalties]1 and correctional imprisonment penalties shall be cumulated, within the limits fixed by the following article. <L 2002-04-17/33, art. 5, 035; In force: 07-05-2002>

(1)	<L 2014-04-10/80, art. 13, 111; In force: indeterminate and at the latest 01-05-2016 (see L 2014-05-08/55, art. 6, itself modified by L 2015-11-23/02, art. 13), art. 13, modified by L 2016-02-05/11, art. 53, 114>

###### Art. 60

<L 01-02-1977, art. 5> In case of concurrence of several offences, the penalties shall be cumulated without, however, being able to exceed twice the maximum of the most severe penalty. [3 The penalty pronounced may not exceed either twenty years' imprisonment, or the most severe imprisonment penalty if the latter is greater than twenty years' imprisonment.]3 [2In no case may this penalty exceed [3 ...]3 one year of electronic monitoring penalty, three hundred hours of work penalty or two years of autonomous probation penalty]2

(2)	<L 2014-04-10/80, art. 14, 111; In force: indeterminate and at the latest 01-05-2016 (see L 2014-05-08/55, art. 6, itself modified by L 2015-11-23/02, art. 13), art. 14 modified by L 2016-02-05/11, art. 54, 114>
(3)	<L 2016-02-05/11, art. 15, 114; In force: 29-02-2016> (NOTE: by its judgment

n° 148/2017 of 21-12-2017 (M.B. 12-01-2018, p. 1393), the Constitutional Court annulled article 15) (See archived version n° 113)

###### Art. 61

Where a crime concurs, either with one or more offences, or with one or more petty offences, the penalty for the crime alone shall be pronounced.

###### Art. 62. (See NOTE under TITLE)

In case of concurrence of several crimes, the most severe penalty alone shall be pronounced. This penalty may even be increased by five years above the maximum, if it consists of (imprisonment for a term or detention of fifteen years to twenty years or a lesser term). <L 2003-01-23/42, art. 14, 040; In force: 13-03-2003>

###### Art. 63. (See NOTE under TITLE)

<L 2003-01-23/42, art. 15, 040; In force: 13-03-2003> The most severe penalty is that whose duration is the longest. If the penalties are of the same duration, imprisonment is considered a more severe penalty than detention.

###### Art. 64

Penalties of special confiscation, by reason of several crimes, offences or petty offences, shall always be cumulated.

###### Art. 65

<L 1994-07-11/33, art. 45, 012; In force: 31-07-1994> Where the same act constitutes several offences or where different offences submitted simultaneously to the same trial judge constitute the successive and continuous manifestation of the same criminal intention, the most severe penalty alone shall be pronounced.
Where the trial judge finds that offences having previously been the subject of a final decision and other acts of which he is seized and which, assuming them to be established, are prior to the said decision and constitute with the former the successive and continuous manifestation of the same criminal intention, he takes into account, for the determination of the penalty, the penalties already pronounced. If these appear to him sufficient for a just punishment of all the offences, he pronounces on the guilt and refers in his decision to the penalties already pronounced. The total of the penalties pronounced in application of this article may not exceed the maximum of the most severe penalty.

#### CHAPTER VII. - PARTICIPATION OF SEVERAL PERSONS IN THE SAME

CRIME OR OFFENCE.

###### Art. 66. Shall be punished as perpetrators of a crime or offence

Those who have executed it or who have directly cooperated in its execution;
Those who, by any act, have provided assistance for the execution such that, without their assistance, the crime or offence could not have been committed;
Those who, by gifts, promises, threats, abuse of authority or power, machinations or culpable artifices, have directly provoked this crime or this offence;
(Those who, either by speeches held in meetings or in public places, or by writings, printed matter, images or emblems of any kind, which have been posted, distributed or sold, offered for sale or exposed to public view, have directly provoked its commission, without prejudice to the penalties provided by law against the perpetrators of provocations to crimes or offences, even in the case where these provocations have not been followed by effect.) <L 28-07-1934, art. 1, I>

###### Art. 67

Shall be punished as accomplices to a crime or offence:
Those who have given instructions to commit it;
Those who have procured weapons, instruments, or any other means which served in the crime or offence, knowing that they were to serve therein;
Those who, outside the case provided for by § 3 of article 66, have, with knowledge, aided or assisted the perpetrator or perpetrators of the crime or offence in the acts which prepared or facilitated it, or in those which consummated it.

###### Art. 68

Those who, knowing the criminal conduct of malefactors exercising brigandage or violence against the security of the State, public peace, persons or property, have habitually provided them with lodging, place of retreat or meeting, shall be punished as their accomplices.

###### Art. 69

Accomplices to a crime shall be punished with the penalty immediately below that which they would incur if they were perpetrators of that crime, in accordance with articles 80 and 81 of this code. [1 They shall however be punished with imprisonment of twenty years to thirty years or detention of twenty years to thirty years if they were accomplices to a crime punishable by life imprisonment or life detention.]1

The penalty pronounced against accomplices to an offence shall not exceed two-thirds of that which would be applied to them if they were perpetrators of that offence.

(1)	<L 2016-02-05/11, art. 16, 114; In force: 29-02-2016>

#### CHAPTER VIII. - GROUNDS OF JUSTIFICATION AND EXCUSE

###### Art. 70

(Except with regard to the offences defined in Book II, Title Ibis, there is no offence), when the act was ordered by law and commanded by authority. <L 2003-08-05/32, art. 3, 044; In force: 07-08-2003>

###### Art. 71

[1 There is no offence when the accused or the defendant was suffering, at the time of the facts, from a mental disorder which abolished [2 ...]2 his capacity for discernment or control of his acts or when he was compelled by a force which he could not resist.]1

###### Art. 78

No crime or offence may be excused, except in the cases determined by law.

#### CHAPTER IX. - MITIGATING CIRCUMSTANCES

###### Art. 79

If there are mitigating circumstances, criminal penalties are reduced or modified in accordance with the following provisions.

###### Art. 80

<L 2001-12-11/50, art. 2, 033; In force: 17-02-2002> Life imprisonment shall be replaced by fixed-term imprisonment or by imprisonment of at least three years [1 and at most forty years]1.
[1 Imprisonment of thirty to forty years by imprisonment of thirty-eight years or for a lesser term or by imprisonment of at least three years and at most thirty-eight years.
Imprisonment of twenty to thirty years, by imprisonment of twenty-eight years or for a lesser term or by imprisonment of at least three years and at most twenty-eight years.]1
Imprisonment of fifteen to twenty years, by imprisonment of ten to fifteen years or of five to ten years or by imprisonment of at least one year [1 and at most fifteen years]1.
Imprisonment of ten to fifteen years, by imprisonment of five to ten years or by imprisonment of at least six months [1 and at most ten years]1.
Imprisonment of five to ten years, by imprisonment of at least one month [1 and at most five years]1.

(1)	<L 2016-02-05/11, art. 17, 114; In force: 29-02-2016> (NOTE: by its judgment no. 148/2017 of 21-12-2017 (M.B. 12-01-2018, p. 1393), the Constitutional Court annulled article 17, 1° and 2° (elements in italics)

###### Art. 81

<L 2003-01-23/42, art. 16, 040; In force: 13-03-2003> Life detention imposed for a crime against the external security of the State shall be replaced by fixed-term detention or by imprisonment of at least one year [1 and at most forty years]1.
[1 The penalty of detention of thirty to forty years by detention of thirty-eight years or for a lesser term or by imprisonment of at least one year and at most thirty-eight years.
The penalty of detention of twenty to thirty years by detention of twenty-eight years or for a lesser term or by imprisonment of at least one year and at most twenty-eight years.]1

The penalty of detention of fifteen to twenty years by detention of ten to fifteen years or of five to ten years or by imprisonment of at least one year [1 and at most fifteen years]1.
The penalty of detention of ten to fifteen years by detention of five to ten years or by imprisonment of at least six months [1 and at most ten years]1. The penalty of detention of five to ten years by imprisonment of at least one month [1 and at most five years]1.

(1)	<L 2016-02-05/11, art. 18, 114; In force: 29-02-2016> (NOTE: by its judgment no. 148/2017 of 21-12-2017 (M.B. 12-01-2018, p. 1393), the Constitutional Court annulled article 18, 1° and 2° (elements in italics))

###### Art. 82

<L 23-08-1919, art. 2> In the cases of concurrence provided for in articles 61 and 62 of the Criminal Code, if, by reason of mitigating circumstances, criminal penalties are reduced to the level of correctional penalties, the trial court may nevertheless impose only a single penalty.

###### Art. 83

The fine in criminal matters may be reduced, without it being in any case less than twenty-six [euros] <L 2000-06-26/42, art. 2, In force: 01-01-2002>.

###### Art. 84

Offenders whose criminal penalty has been commuted to imprisonment may be sentenced to a fine of twenty-six [euros] to one thousand [euros] <L 2000-06-26/42, art. 2, In force: 01-01-2002>.
[2 ...]2
(Paragraph 3 repealed) <L 09-04-1930, art. 32>

###### Art. 85

(If there are mitigating circumstances, [3 the penalties of imprisonment, [4 ...]4 work penalties, autonomous probation penalties and fine penalties may respectively be reduced below eight days, [4 ...]4 forty-five hours, twelve months and twenty-six euros]3, without them being less than police penalties.) <L 2002-04-17/33, art. 7, 035; In force: 07-05-2002>

The judges may also apply one or the other of these penalties separately.
If imprisonment is imposed alone, the judges may substitute a fine which shall not exceed five hundred [euros] <L 2000-06-26/42, art. 2, In force: 01-01-2002>.
If the deprivation of the rights enumerated in [1 article 31, paragraph 1]1 (...) is ordered or authorized, the judges may impose these penalties for a term of one to five years, or remit them entirely. <L 09-04-1930, art. 32>

#### CHAPTER X. - EXTINCTION OF PENALTIES

###### Art. 86

Penalties imposed by judgments or decisions that have become final are extinguished by the death of the convicted person. (The loss of legal personality of the convicted legal person does not extinguish the penalty.) <L 1999-05-04/60, art. 11, 024; In force: 02-07-1999>

###### Art. 87

The incapacities imposed by judges or attached by law to certain convictions cease by the remission which the King may grant, by virtue of the right of pardon.

<L 09-04-1930, art. 32>

###### Art. 91

(Except for penalties concerning the offences defined in articles 136bis, 136ter and 136quater, criminal penalties shall be subject to limitation) by twenty full years, counting from the date of the judgments or decisions imposing them. <L 2003-08-05/32, art. , 044; In force: 07-08-2003>

###### Art. 92

[1 Except for sentences concerning the offences defined in articles 136bis, 136ter and 136quater, which are not subject to limitation, correctional sentences shall be subject to limitation after five full years]1 have elapsed, from the date of the judgment or decision rendered in final instance, or from the day on which the judgment rendered in first instance can no longer be challenged by way of appeal.
If the sentence imposed exceeds three years, the limitation period shall be ten years.
[1 If the sentence of imprisonment imposed exceeds twenty years, the limitation period shall be twenty years.]1

###### Art. 93

Police sentences shall be subject to limitation after one full year has elapsed, from the times fixed in the preceding article.

###### Art. 94

[1 Fines shall be subject to limitation within the periods fixed by the preceding articles, according to whether they are imposed for crimes, offences or contraventions.
Special confiscations shall be subject to limitation within the periods fixed by the preceding articles, according to whether they are imposed for contraventions or crimes.
Special confiscations imposed for offences shall be subject to limitation after ten full years have elapsed, from the times determined in article 92.]1

(1)	<L 2014-02-11/12, art. 48, 100; En vigueur : 18-04-2014>

###### Art. 95

If the convicted person who was serving his sentence has succeeded in escaping, the limitation period begins to run from the day of the escape.
However, in this case, the time during which the convicted person served his sentence beyond five years, if it is a temporary criminal sentence, or beyond two years, if it is a correctional sentence, shall be deducted from the duration of the limitation period.

###### Art. 96

The limitation of the sentence shall be interrupted by the arrest of the convicted person.

###### §

1er. The limitation of confiscation is suspended when the law so provides or when there is a legal impediment to the immediate execution of this sentence.

###### § 2

The limitation is in any case suspended in the following cases: 1° while the convicted person is subject to legal collective insolvency
proceedings;
2° during the processing of the application for pardon concerning the confiscation incurred, submitted by the convicted person or third parties in accordance with articles 110 and 111 of the Constitution;
3° during the duration of a settlement plan granted to the convicted person by the competent official of the federal public service Finance responsible for the recovery of the confiscation, fine or legal costs.]1

###### §

1er. The limitation of confiscation is interrupted by any act of execution emanating from the legally competent authorities.

###### § 2. The limitation is in any case interrupted in the following cases

1° any partial payment made by or for the convicted person to the competent official of the federal public service Finance responsible for the recovery of the confiscation that does not fall within the framework of a settlement plan granted by the receiver;
2° any request for payment or any formal notice addressed to the convicted person, by registered mail or by bailiff's writ, and emanating from the competent official of the federal public service Finance responsible for the recovery of the confiscation;
3° any seizure carried out by or at the request of the competent official of the federal public service Finance responsible for the recovery of the confiscation;
4° the decision of the director of the Central Organ for Seizure and Confiscation to investigate the solvency of the convicted person;
5° the decision of the public prosecutor's office to open a criminal enforcement investigation in accordance with article 464/1 of the Code of Criminal Procedure;

6° all acts of execution performed within the framework of the criminal enforcement investigation in accordance with article 464/1 of the Code of Criminal Procedure.]1

Civil convictions, pronounced by judgments or decisions rendered in criminal, correctional or police matters, shall be subject to limitation according to the rules of civil law, from the day on which they become irrevocable.

## LIVRE 2. - DES INFRACTIONS ET DE LEUR REPRESSION EN PARTICULIER

### TITRE I. - DES CRIMES ET DES DELITS CONTRE LA SURETE DE L'ETAT

#### CHAPITRE I

DES ATTENTATS ET DES COMPLOTS CONTRE LE ROI, CONTRE LA FAMILLE ROYALE ET CONTRE LA FORME DU GOUVERNEMENT.

###### Art. 101

An attempt on the life or person of the King shall be punished (by imprisonment for life). <L 1996-07-10/42, art. 3, 018; En vigueur : 11-08-1996> If it has not had the result of infringing the liberty of the King, and if it has caused him neither bloodshed, nor wound, nor illness, the attempt on his person shall be punished (by imprisonment of twenty to thirty years). <L 2003-01-23/42, art. 17, 040; En vigueur : 13-03-2003>

###### Art. 102

<L 2003-01-23/42, art. 18, 040; En vigueur : 13-03-2003> An attempt on the life of the heir presumptive to the crown shall be punished by imprisonment for life.
An attempt on his person shall be punished by imprisonment of twenty years to thirty years. If it has not had the result of infringing his liberty and if it has caused him neither bloodshed, nor wound, nor illness, the attempt on his person shall be punished by imprisonment of fifteen years to twenty years.

###### Art. 103. (See NOTE under TITLE)

An attempt against the life of the Queen, of the King's relatives and in-laws in direct line, of the King's brothers, having Belgian nationality, against the life of the Regent, or against the life of the ministers exercising, in the cases provided for by the

Constitution, the constitutional powers of the King, shall always be punished as the completed act.
(An attempt against their person shall be punished by imprisonment of ten years to fifteen years; it shall be punished by imprisonment of five years to ten years, if it did not result in affecting their freedom and if it caused them neither bloodshed, nor injury, nor illness.) <L 2003-01-23/42, art. 19, 040; In force: 13-03-2003>

###### Art. 104. (See NOTE under TITLE)
