An attempt the purpose of which is either to destroy or to change the form of government or the order of succession to the throne, or to cause citizens or inhabitants to take up arms against the royal authority, the Legislative Chambers or one of them, shall be punished by detention (of twenty years to thirty years). <L 2003-01-23/42, art. 20, 040; In force: 13-03-2003>

###### Art. 105. An attempt exists as soon as there is a punishable attempt

###### Art. 106. (See NOTE under TITLE)

A conspiracy against the life or against the person of the King shall be punished by fifteen years to twenty years of (imprisonment), if it has been followed by an act committed to prepare its execution, and by ten years to fifteen years of the same penalty, in the contrary case. <L 2003-01-23/42, art. 21, 040; In force: 13-03-2003>

###### Art. 107. (See NOTE under TITLE)

<L 2003-01-23/42, art. 22, 040; In force: 13-03-2003> A conspiracy against the life or against the person of the heir presumptive to the crown shall be punished by ten years to fifteen years of imprisonment, if it has been followed by an act committed to prepare its execution, and by five years to ten years of imprisonment, in the contrary case.

###### Art. 108. (See NOTE under TITLE)

A conspiracy against the life or against the person, either of the members of the royal family enumerated in article 103, or of the Regent, or of the ministers exercising the constitutional powers of the King, shall be punished by (imprisonment of five years to ten years). <L 2003-01-23/42, art. 23, 040; In force: 13-03-2003>

###### Art. 109. (See NOTE under TITLE)

A conspiracy formed to achieve one of the purposes mentioned in article 104 shall be punished by ten years to fifteen years of detention, if any act has been committed to prepare its execution, and by five years to ten years

of the same penalty, in the contrary case.

###### Art. 110

There is a conspiracy as soon as the resolution to act has been agreed upon between several persons.

###### Art. 111

The proposal made and not accepted to form a conspiracy against the life or against the person of the King, of the heir presumptive to the crown, of the members of the Royal Family enumerated in article 103, of the Regent, or of the ministers exercising the constitutional powers of the King, shall be punished by imprisonment of one year to five years.
The guilty party (...) may, in addition, be sentenced to disqualification, in accordance with article 33. <L 09-04-1930, art. 32>

###### Art. 112. (See NOTE under TITLE)

Whoever has formed alone the resolution to commit an attempt against the life or against the person of the King, of the heir presumptive to the crown, of the members of the Royal Family enumerated in article 103, of the Regent or of the ministers exercising the constitutional powers of the King, shall be punished by (imprisonment of five years to ten years), when he has committed an act to prepare its execution. <L 2003-01-23/42, art. 23, 040; In force: 13-03-2003>

#### CHAPTER II. - CRIMES AND OFFENCES AGAINST THE EXTERNAL SECURITY OF THE STATE

###### Art. 113

(Any Belgian who has borne arms against Belgium shall be punished (by detention for life).) <AL 11-10-1916, art. 1> <L 1996-07-10/42, art. 15,
018; In force: 11-08-1996>
(For the application of this provision, constitutes the act of bearing arms against Belgium, that of knowingly performing for the enemy combat, transport, work or surveillance tasks, which normally fall to the enemy armies or their services.) <AL 17-12-1942, art. 1>

###### Art. 114

<L 2003-01-23/42, art. 24, 040; In force: 13-03-2003> Whoever has engaged in machinations or maintained intelligence with a foreign power or with any person acting in the interest of a foreign power, to induce that power to undertake war

against Belgium, or to provide it with the means to do so, shall be punished by detention of twenty years to thirty years. If hostilities have ensued he shall be punished by detention for life.

###### Art. 115

§ 1. (Shall be punished (by detention for life): <L 1996-07-10/42, art. 15, 018; In force: 11-08-1996>
He who has facilitated the entry into the territory of the kingdom for the enemies of the State; He who has delivered to them cities, fortresses, stores, arsenals, vessels or buildings belonging to Belgium;
He who has provided them with assistance in soldiers, men, money, provisions, arms or munitions;
He who has assisted the progress of their arms on the territory of the kingdom or against the Belgian land or sea forces, by undermining the loyalty of officers, soldiers, sailors or other citizens towards the King and the State.
In the above cases, the punishable attempt shall be treated as the crime itself.
The conspiracy having as its purpose one of these crimes shall be punished by (detention of twenty years to thirty years), if it has been followed by an act committed to prepare its execution, and by detention of five years to ten years, in the contrary case.) <L 10-12-1937, art. unique, 2°> <L 1996-07-10/42, art. 3, 018; In force: 11-08-1996> <L 2003-01-
23/42, art. 25, 040; In force: 13-03-2003>

###### § 2

(The provision of paragraph 4 of § 1 is applicable to one who resides in territory occupied by the enemy, only:
1° If, either directly, or through an intermediary or in that capacity, he has provided the enemies of the State with assistance in soldiers, men, money, provisions intended for the supply of the enemy, offensive or defensive war material, munitions of war properly so-called, spare parts intended for the manufacture of that material or those munitions, clothing or equipment items that he knew to be for military use, or if, for them, he has organized or directed an enterprise of works for the establishment, fitting out or camouflage of fortifications, aerodromes or any other constructions or installations for military purposes;
2° If, either directly, or through an intermediary or in that capacity, he has provided them with raw materials, materials or products that he knew were intended for the manufacture of that material, those munitions or those items, or for the execution of those works, unless he made those supplies by using all means at his disposal to resist the execution of the orders of the enemies of the State;

3° If, either directly, or through an intermediary or in that capacity, he has provided them with raw or manufactured materials, products, foodstuffs or animals, when that supply has been consecutive to solicitations or approaches made to them or to intermediaries acting on their behalf or when it has necessitated the creation, transformation or enlargement of the enterprise or the modification of its nature or its methods of operation, or when production has been maintained or brought to an abnormal level to satisfy their orders, or when the supplier has had recourse to their help to settle social conflicts or he has organized counter-sabotage services;
4° If he has put his activity at their service with a view to gathering, on their behalf, the raw or manufactured materials, products, foodstuffs or animals referred to in 1°, 2° and 3° above.) <AL 25-05-1945, art. 1>


This excerpt sits under: PENAL CODE > BOOK 2. - OFFENCES AND THEIR PUNISHMENT IN PARTICULAR > TITLE I. - CRIMES AND OFFENCES AGAINST THE SECURITY OF THE STATE > CHAPTER I

###### Art. 116

<L 19-07-1934, art. 1> Whoever has knowingly delivered or communicated in whole or in part, in original or in reproduction, to an enemy power or to any person acting in the interest of an enemy power, objects, plans, writings, documents or information the secrecy of which vis-à-vis the enemy concerns the defence of the territory or the security of the State, shall be punished (with detention for life). <L 1996-07-10/42, art. 15, 018; En vigueur
: 11-08-1996>

###### Art. 117

(The penalties expressed in articles 113, 115 and 116 shall be the same, whether the crimes provided for by these articles have been committed against Belgium, or whether they have been committed against the allies of Belgium acting against the common enemy.) <AL 11-10-1916, art. 1>
(For the application of this provision, an ally of Belgium is any State which, even independently of a treaty of alliance, pursues the war against a State with which Belgium itself is at war.) <AL 17-12-1942, art. 2>

###### Art. 118. (Voir NOTE sous TITRE)

<L 19-07-1934, art. 1> Whoever has knowingly delivered or communicated, in whole or in part, in original or in reproduction, to a foreign power or to any person acting in the interest of a foreign power, objects, plans, writings, documents or information, the secrecy of which concerns the defence of the territory or the external security of the State, shall be punished with (detention of ten to fifteen years.)
If the offender was vested with a public function or mandate or if he

performed a mission or accomplished work entrusted to him by the Government, he shall be punished with (detention of fifteen to twenty years). <L 2003-01-23/42, art. 26, 040; En vigueur : 13-03-2003>

Art. 118bis. <AL 17-12-1942, art. 3> Shall be punished (with detention for life), whoever has participated in the transformation by the enemy of legal institutions or organizations, undermined in time of war the loyalty of citizens towards the King and the State, or who has knowingly served the policy or designs of the enemy.
<L 1996-07-10/42, art. 15, 018; En vigueur : 11-08-1996>
Shall likewise be punished (with detention for life), whoever has knowingly directed, practised by any means whatsoever, provoked, aided or favoured propaganda directed against resistance to the enemy or to its allies or tending to the acts enumerated in the preceding paragraph. <L 1996-07-10/42, art. 15, 018; En vigueur
: 11-08-1996>

###### Art. 119

<L 19-07-1934, art. 1> Whoever has knowingly delivered or communicated, in whole or in part, in original or in reproduction, to any person not qualified to take delivery or knowledge thereof, objects, plans, writings, documents or information referred to in article 118, shall be punished with imprisonment of six months to five years and a fine of 500 to 5,000 [euros]
<L 2000-06-26/42, art. 2, En vigueur : 01-01-2002>.
Shall be punished with the same penalties, whoever, without authorization from the competent authority, has reproduced, published or disclosed, in whole or in part, by any process whatsoever, objects, plans, writings, documents or information referred to in article 118.

###### Art. 120

<L 19-07-1934, art. 1> Whoever, without authority to take delivery or knowledge thereof, has obtained, in whole or in part, in original or in reproduction, objects, plans, writings, documents or information referred to in article 118 or has received them voluntarily, shall be punished by imprisonment of one month to five years and a fine of 100 to 5,000 [euros] <L 2000-06-26/42, art. 2, In force: 01-01-2002>.

Art. 120bis. <L 19-07-1934, art. 1> Shall be punished by imprisonment of six months to five years and a fine of 500 to 5,000 [euros] <L 2000-06-26/42, art. 2, In force: 01-01-2002>:

1° Whoever, in disguise or by concealing his identity, profession, capacity or nationality, or by means of a manoeuvre intended to deceive the agents assigned to guard or to evade their surveillance, has entered either a fort, any defence work, a post, a State vessel, or a vessel requisitioned or chartered by it, a military, maritime or aeronautical establishment, a military depot, magazine or park, or a workshop, shipyard or laboratory where work concerning the defence of the territory is being carried out for the State;
2° Whoever, by one of the means provided for in the preceding paragraph, has drawn up a plan, reconnoitred communication routes, means of correspondence or remote transmission or gathered information concerning the defence of the territory or the external security of the State;
3° Whoever, with a view to gathering or transmitting information concerning the defence of the territory or the external security of the State and without having authority to that effect, has organised or employed any means of correspondence or remote transmission.

Art. 120ter. <L 19-07-1934, art. 1> Shall be punished by imprisonment of eight days to (one year) and a fine of 26 to 100 [euros]: <L 10-12-1937, art. unique, 4°> <L 2000-06-26/42, art. 2, In force: 01-01-2002>
1° Whoever, without authorisation from the military, maritime or aeronautical authority, has carried out by any process surveys or topographical operations within a radius of one myriametre or within any other radius that shall subsequently be fixed by the Minister of National Defence, around a fortified place, a defence work, a post, a military or maritime establishment, an aeronautical establishment other than an aerodrome or air terminal, a military depot, magazine or park, from the advanced works, or has taken photographs of one of these places, works or establishments, published, exhibited, sold or distributed reproductions of these views;
2° Whoever, without authorisation, has climbed or crossed either the revetments or slopes of fortifications, or the walls, barriers, railings, palisades, hedges or other enclosures, established on military land or has entered a fort or one of the other establishments referred to in article 120bis, 1°.

Art. 120quater. <L 19-07-1934, art. 1> The attempt of one of the offences provided for by articles 116, 119, 120 to 120ter is considered as the offence

itself.

Art. 120quinquies. <L 19-07-1934, art. 1> Shall be punished by imprisonment of one month to one year and a fine of 100 to 1,000 [euros], whoever, contrary to the regulations, has moved or held objects, plans, writings or documents referred to in article 118, or whoever, through negligence or non-observance of the regulations, has allowed to be destroyed, removed or taken away, even temporarily, all or part of these objects, plans, writings or documents which have been entrusted to him or of which he has had knowledge by reason of his functions, his status, his profession, a mission, a mandate or has allowed knowledge, copy or reproduction thereof to be taken by any process, in whole or in part. <L 2000-06-26/42, art. 2, In force: 01-01-2002>

Art. 120sexies. <L 19-07-1934, art. 1> If they have been committed in time of war:
The offences provided for by articles 118, 119, 120 and 120bis shall be punished by (detention for life); <L 1996-07-10/42, art. 15, 018; In force: 11-08-1996>
The offences provided for by article 120ter shall be punished by (detention of fifteen years to twenty years); <L 2003-01-23/42, art. 27, 040; In force: 13-03-2003> The offences provided for by article 120quinquies shall be punished by imprisonment of six months to five years and a fine of 500 to 5,000 [euros]
<L 2000-06-26/42, art. 2, In force: 01-01-2002>.

Art. 120septies. <L 19-07-1934, art. 1> Without prejudice to the application of articles 66 and 67, shall be punished by imprisonment of eight days to six months and a fine of 26 to 500 [euros], whoever, knowing the intentions of the perpetrators of one of the offences provided for by articles 120 or 120bis or of the attempt of one of these offences, has provided them with lodging, place of retreat or meeting, has either received or transmitted their correspondence, or has concealed the objects or instruments having served or to serve to commit the offence. <L 2000-06-26/42, art. 2, In force: 01-01-2002>

Art. 120octies. <L 19-03-1956, art. unique> The penalties expressed in articles 118, 119, 120 to 120septies shall be the same whether the offences provided for by these articles have been committed against Belgium, or whether they have been committed against

a State with which Belgium is united by a regional agreement with a view to a common defence.

###### Art. 121

<L 2003-01-23/42, art. 28, 040; In force: 13-03-2003> Whoever has concealed or caused to be concealed spies or enemy soldiers sent on reconnaissance, and whom he has known as such, shall be punished by detention for life. Whoever has concealed or caused to be concealed enemy agents or soldiers, able-bodied or wounded, or who has come to their aid to enable them to evade the authorities shall be punished by detention of fifteen years to twenty years. In a state of siege, the offence shall be punished by detention for life.
Whoever has concealed or caused to be concealed a subject of an enemy power or allied to the enemy or who has come to his aid to enable him to evade the authorities, shall be punished by detention of five years to ten years. In a state of siege, the offence shall be punished by detention of ten years to fifteen years.
Whoever has concealed or caused to be concealed persons whom he knew to be prosecuted or convicted for one of the offences provided for in Chapter II, of Title 1, of Book II of the Penal Code and in articles 17 and 18 of the law of 27 May 1870 containing the Military Penal Code, or who has come to their aid to enable them to evade the action of justice, shall be punished by the penalty provided for that offence, without the penalty imposed being able to exceed fifteen years of detention or imprisonment.
Excepted from the provision provided for in the preceding paragraph are the ascendants or descendants, spouses or wives, even divorced, brothers or sisters and relatives in the same degrees of the perpetrators or accomplices of the offences in question.

Art. 121bis.<L 2003-01-23/42, art. 29, 040; In force: 13-03-2003> Shall be punished by detention of five years to ten years, whoever has knowingly, by the denunciation of a real or imaginary fact, exposed any person to the searches, prosecutions or rigours of the enemy.
He shall be punished by detention of ten years to fifteen years if the denunciation has resulted, for any person and without the intervention of a new denunciation, in a deprivation of liberty of more than one month.
He shall be punished by detention for life if, following detention or treatment undergone, the denunciation has had as a consequence for any person and without the intervention of a new denunciation, either death, or an apparently incurable illness, or [1personal incapacity for work of more than four

months]1, or the loss of absolute use of an organ, or serious mutilation.

(1)<L 2016-02-05/11, art. 20, 114; In force: 29-02-2016>


This excerpt sits under: PENAL CODE > BOOK 2. - OFFENCES AND THEIR PUNISHMENT IN PARTICULAR > TITLE I. - CRIMES AND OFFENCES AGAINST THE SECURITY OF THE STATE > CHAPTER II. - CRIMES AND OFFENCES AGAINST THE EXTERNAL SECURITY OF THE STATE

###### Art. 122

<L 2003-01-23/42, art. 30, 040; En vigueur : 13-03-2003> Where objects have been set on fire or destroyed by any means whatsoever, with the intention of aiding the enemy, the penalties provided for such acts by Chapter III, Title IX shall be replaced by:
imprisonment, by imprisonment for ten to fifteen years;
imprisonment for five to ten years, by imprisonment for fifteen to twenty years; imprisonment for ten to fifteen years, by imprisonment for twenty to thirty years; imprisonment for fifteen years or more, by life imprisonment;
the attempt to set fire or destroy shall be considered as the crime itself.

Art. 122bis. <AL 31-12-1939, art. 1> Without prejudice to the application of more severe provisions, any person who has established or operated a military intelligence service functioning on the territory of the kingdom in the interest and to the detriment of foreign powers, any person who has exercised any activity whatsoever in such a service, in particular either by recruiting collaborators or agents for it, or by delivering or communicating knowingly to it, in whole or in part, in original or in reproduction, objects, plans, writings, documents or information not manifestly public concerning the military organization or defence arrangements of a foreign power, the supply of food, arms or ammunition to its land, sea or air forces or the equipment in use therein, or by transmitting the said objects, plans, writings, documents or information to another foreign power or to a person acting in the interest thereof, shall be punished by imprisonment of six months to five years and a fine of one hundred to five thousand [euros]. <L 2000-06-26/42, art. 2,En vigueur
: 01-01-2002>

###### Art. 123

Any person who, by hostile actions not approved by the government, has exposed the State to hostilities on the part of a foreign power, shall be punished by detention for five to ten years, and if hostilities have ensued, by detention for ten to fifteen years.

Art. 123bis. <L 19-07-1934, art. 1> Without prejudice to the application of Article 1 of the law of 7 July 1875, Articles 66 and 67 of this Code, the following shall be punished by imprisonment of eight days to (three years) and a fine of 50 to 1,000 [euros]: <L 10-12-1937, art. unique, 5°> <L 2000-06-26/42, art. 2, En vigueur : 01-01-2002>
1° The offer or proposal to commit one of the offences provided for by Articles 113 to 120bis, 121 to 123;
2° The acceptance of such offer or proposal.

Art. 123ter. (If the offences provided for by Articles 115 to 120quater, 120sexies to 123bis, have been committed for profit, (the sums, goods or any direct or indirect advantages whatsoever which constitute the profit resulting from the activity of the offender, or, where they have not been seized, the amount of their value), shall be declared forfeited to the Treasury.) <L 19-07-1934, art. 1> <L 07-06-1948, art. 1>
(In the same case, the penalties of imprisonment provided for by Articles 119 and 120 shall be replaced by imprisonment for five to ten years and detention for a fixed term by imprisonment for a fixed term of the same duration.) <L 2003-01-23/42, art. 31,
040; En vigueur : 13-03-2003>
(If there are mitigating circumstances, (life imprisonment) shall be replaced in accordance with Article 80.) <L 19-07-1934, art. 1> <L 1996-07-10/42, art. 15, 018; En vigueur : 11-08-1996>

Art. 123quater. (Voir NOTE sous TITRE) <L 19-07-1934, art. 1> Without prejudice to the application of more severe provisions, any conspiracy to commit a crime or offence against persons or property, formed with the intention of hindering, in time of war, either the defence of the territory, or mobilization, or the supply of food, arms or ammunition to the army, shall be punished by the penalties provided for by Article 123bis.
If the conspiracy is formed in time of war, it shall be punished by (imprisonment for five to ten years). <L 2003-01-23/42, art. 31, 040; En vigueur : 13-03-2003>

Art. 123quinquies.<L 19-07-1934, art. 1> The confiscation of things which have been used or which have been intended to commit the offence shall always be ordered, as well as the confiscation of plans, maps, writings, documents, copies, surveys, photographs, views, reproductions and all other things obtained by the offence.

(In the cases provided for in Articles 119, 120, 120bis, 121bis, 122bis and 123quater, offenders sentenced to imprisonment may be sentenced to permanent or temporary deprivation of the rights listed in [1 Article 31, paragraph 1]1.) <AL 31-12-1939, art. 2>

(1)<L 2009-04-14/01, art. 8, 073; En vigueur : 15-04-2009>

Art. 123sexies. <L 30-06-1961, art. 1> § 1. (By way of derogation from Articles 31 and 32, judgments or decisions sentencing to life imprisonment or life detention, to imprisonment for ten to fifteen years or a higher term or detention for twenty to thirty years or fifteen to twenty years for an offence or attempted offence provided for in Chapter II, Title 1, Book II of the Criminal Code, committed in time of war, shall not order against the convicted persons the deprivation of the rights referred to therein, but shall entail by operation of law permanent disqualification from:) <L 2003-01-23/42, art. 33, 040; En vigueur : 13-03-2003>
1° the rights listed in the said Article 31 including the rights to vote, to elect, to be elected;
2° the right to be registered on one of the rolls of the bar, on a list of honorary lawyers or on a list of trainee lawyers;
3° the right to participate in any capacity whatsoever in teaching given in a public or private establishment;
4° the right to be remunerated by the State as a minister of a religion; 5° the right to be a leader of a political association;
6° the right to participate in any capacity whatsoever in the operation, administration, editing, printing or distribution of a newspaper or any publication in cases where such participation has a political character;
7° the right to participate in the management or administration of any cultural, philanthropic and sporting event or any public entertainment in cases where such participation has a political character;
8° the right to participate in the operation, administration or in any manner whatsoever in the activity of any enterprise having as its object theatrical performances, cinematography or broadcasting in cases where such participation has a political character;
9° the right to participate in any capacity whatsoever in the administration, management or direction of a professional association or a non-profit association.

###### § 2

By way of derogation from articles 32 and 33, judgments or rulings of conviction to other criminal penalties or to correctional penalties for an offence or attempted offence provided for in chapter II of title I of book II of the Criminal Code, committed in time of war, may pronounce not the prohibition of rights provided for in the said articles but the temporary forfeiture of the rights enumerated in the preceding paragraph.
The forfeitures inscribed in the electoral laws, including (articles 6 and 7 of the Electoral Code), are in any case applicable. <L 05-07-1976, art. 145>
(The forfeitures may be pronounced for a period of ten years to twenty years if the penalty is imprisonment of five years to ten years or detention of five years to ten years or of ten years to fifteen years and for a period of five years to ten years if the penalty is correctional). The duration of the forfeitures fixed by the judgment or ruling of conviction shall run from the day on which the conviction in adversarial proceedings or by default has acquired the force of res judicata. <L 2003-01-23/42, art. 33, 040; In force: 13-03-2003>

Art. 123septies. (See NOTE under TITLE) <L 30-06-1961, art. 1> § 1. Convicted persons subject to forfeiture by application of article 123sexies may request restitution of the rights enumerated under 6° to 9° on condition that:
1° they are not detained in execution of the penalty, nor fugitives or absconders; 2° they have discharged the pecuniary penalties pronounced against them and have freed themselves from the restitutions, damages and costs to which they have been sentenced; however, the court may exempt from this condition the convicted person who proves that he was in the impossibility of freeing himself, either by reason of his indigence, or by reason of any other cause not attributable to him;
3° since the day on which the forfeiture took effect there has elapsed a period of twenty years if the convicted person has been subject to forfeiture in perpetuity, of ten years if he has been subject to forfeiture of ten to twenty years following a conviction to (imprisonment of five years to ten years or to detention of five years to ten years or of ten years to fifteen years), and of five years if he has been subject to a forfeiture of five to ten years following a conviction to a correctional penalty. <L 2003-01-23/42, art. 34, 040; In force: 13-03-2003>

###### § 2

The application is addressed by registered letter to the King's Prosecutor of the domicile or residence of the interested party and, if the latter has in Belgium neither domicile nor certain residence, to that of the district of Brussels.
The King's Prosecutor takes all information he deems necessary and brings the

application before the Court of First Instance.
The interested party appears before the court sitting in council chamber either in person, or by a solicitor or by a lawyer bearing the documents, upon simple summons addressed to him, under registered cover by post, by the King's Prosecutor.
This summons contains the indication of the Chamber of the court before which the application will be brought and of the day and hour of appearance. There shall be at least a period of eight days between the notification and the day of appearance. The delivery of the registered cover to the post constitutes notification.
If, upon notification, the interested party does not appear either in person, or by solicitor, or by lawyer bearing the documents, the court may, before ruling on the application, adjourn the case in order to enable the public prosecutor to address a new summons to him.
The file of the public prosecutor is deposited at the registry of the court at least eight days before the fixed hearing. The procedure continues at the hearing as in correctional matters.
The judgment rendered on the application is without appeal.
If the application is rejected in whole or in part, it may not be renewed before the expiry of two years from the date of the judicial decision.
In case of death of the interested party, the remedies and applications provided for in this law may be pursued by his spouse, his descendants, his ascendants or his brothers and sisters.
They may also be pursued by one or more successors in title universal or particular who will prove a pecuniary interest.

###### § 3

The restitution of the rights of which the convicted persons had been deprived by application of the preceding article has effect only for the future.

Art. 123octies. <L 30-06-1961, art. 1> When the judgment or ruling entailing the forfeiture in perpetuity or for a time of rights by application of article 123sexies has become res judicata, the Public Prosecutor has it published by extract in the "Moniteur belge" with mention of the forfeiture pronounced or resulting therefrom. In addition, he notifies it by extract to the civil registrar of the last domicile with a view to the inscription of this same mention in the population register. This mention is reproduced in the population register of any new domicile.

Art. 123nonies. <L 30-06-1961, art. 1> Whoever in spite of the forfeiture resulting from the application of §§ 1 or 2 of article 123sexies makes, either directly or through the interposition of a person, use of one of the rights enumerated in that article, is punished by imprisonment of one year to three years and a fine of ten thousand to one hundred thousand [euros]. <L 2000-06-26/42, art. 2, In force: 01-01-2002>

Art. 123decies. <AL 20-09-1945, art. 2> <L 01-06-1949, art. 1, 4°> Companies are civilly liable for convictions to damages, fines, costs, confiscations, restitutions and pecuniary sanctions of any kind pronounced for offences against the provisions of this chapter against their organs or agents.
The same applies to members of all commercial associations lacking legal personality, when the offence has been committed by a partner, manager or agent, on the occasion of an operation falling within the scope of the activity of the association. The partner civilly liable is however personally liable only up to the amount of the sums or values he has derived from the operation.
These companies and partners may be cited directly before the criminal jurisdiction by the public prosecutor or the civil party.

#### CHAPTER III. - CRIMES AGAINST THE INTERNAL SECURITY OF THE STATE

###### Art. 124. (See NOTE under TITLE)

The attempt the purpose of which is to incite civil war, by arming or by inducing citizens or inhabitants to arm themselves against one another, shall be punished by (detention of fifteen years to twenty years). <L 2003-01-23/42, art. 35, 040; In force: 13-03-2003>
The conspiracy formed for the same purpose shall be punished by ten years to fifteen years of detention, if any act has been committed to prepare its execution; and by five years to ten years of the same penalty, in the contrary case.

###### Art. 125. (See NOTE under TITLE)

<L 2003-01-23/42, art. 36, 040; In force: 13-03-2003> The attempt the purpose of which is to bring devastation, massacre or pillage in one or more communes, shall be punished by fifteen years to twenty years of imprisonment.
The conspiracy formed for the same purpose shall be punished by ten years to fifteen years of the same penalty, if any act has been committed to prepare its execution; and by

five years to ten years of the same penalty, in the contrary case.

###### Art. 126. (See NOTE under TITLE)

Shall be punished by detention of five years to ten years, those who have raised or caused to be raised armed troops, engaged or enlisted, caused to be engaged or enlisted soldiers, or have furnished or procured for them either arms or munitions, without order or authorization of the government.

###### Art. 127. (See NOTE under TITLE) Shall be punished by detention from five years to ten years

Those who, without right or legitimate reason, shall have taken command of an army corps, a troop, a warship, a fortress, a post, a port, a city;
Those who shall have retained, against the order of the government, any military command whatsoever;
Commanders who shall have kept their army or troop assembled, after the disbandment or separation thereof shall have been ordered.

###### Art. 128. (See NOTE under TITLE)

Whoever, whether to seize public funds, or to invade domains, properties, places, cities, fortresses, posts, warehouses, arsenals, ports, vessels or buildings belonging to the State, or to make attack or resistance against the public force acting against the perpetrators of these crimes, shall have placed himself at the head of armed bands, or shall have exercised therein any function or command whatsoever, shall be punished by (detention from fifteen years to twenty years). <L 2003-01-23/42, art. 37, 040; In force: 13-03-2003>

###### Art. 129. (See NOTE under TITLE)

If these bands have had for their purpose, whether to pillage or to divide public or national properties, or those of a generality of citizens, or to make attack or resistance against the public force acting against the perpetrators of these crimes, those who shall have placed themselves at the head of these bands, or who shall have exercised therein any function or command whatsoever, shall be punished by (imprisonment) from fifteen years to twenty years. <L 2003-01-23/42, art. 38,
040; In force: 13-03-2003>

###### Art. 130

The penalties respectively established in the two preceding articles shall be applicable to those who shall have directed the association, raised or caused to be raised, organized or caused to be organized the bands.

###### Art. 131

In the case where one of the crimes mentioned in articles 101, 102, 103 and 104 shall have been committed by a band, the penalties imposed by those articles shall be applied, without distinction of rank, to all individuals forming part of the band and who shall have been seized at the place of the seditious assembly.
Shall be punished by the same penalties, although not seized at the place, whoever shall have directed the sedition or exercised in the band any employment or command whatsoever.

###### Art. 132

Except in the case where the seditious assembly shall have had for its object or result one of the crimes set forth in articles 101, 102, 103 and 104, the individuals forming part of the bands referred to above, without exercising therein any command or employment, and who shall have been seized at the places, shall be punished by the penalty immediately inferior to that which shall be pronounced against the directors or commanders of these bands.

###### Art. 133. (See NOTE under TITLE)

Those who, knowing the purpose or character of the said bands, shall have provided to these bands or to their divisions, lodgings, retreats or places of assembly, shall be punished, in the cases of articles 101, 102, 103 and 128, by (imprisonment from five years to ten years), and, in the cases provided for by articles 104 and 127, by detention from five years to ten years. <L 2003-01-23/42, art.
39, 040; In force: 13-03-2003>

###### Art. 134

No penalty shall be pronounced, for the act of sedition, against those who, having formed part of these bands without exercising therein any command and without fulfilling therein any employment or function, shall have withdrawn at the first warning of the civil or military authorities, or even thereafter, when they shall have been seized outside the places of the seditious assembly, without opposing resistance and without arms. Nevertheless, they shall be punished by reason of the other crimes or offences which they shall have personally committed.

###### Art. 135

Included in the word "arms" are all machines, all instruments, utensils or other cutting, piercing or blunt objects, which one shall have seized to kill, wound or strike, even if one has not made use thereof.

Art. 135bis.<L 20-07-1939, art. unique> Whoever, directly or indirectly, receives from a foreign person or organization and in

whatever form, gifts, presents, loans or other advantages, intended or employed in whole or in part to conduct or to remunerate in Belgium an activity or propaganda of a nature to harm the integrity, sovereignty or independence of the kingdom, or to undermine the loyalty which citizens owe to the State and to the institutions of the Belgian people, is punished by imprisonment from six months to five years, and by a fine from one thousand [euros] to twenty thousand [euros] <L 2000-06-26/42, art. 2, In force: 01-01-2002>.
In all cases of offence, the things received are confiscated [2 ...]2. The prohibition of the exercise of the rights enumerated in [1 article 31, paragraph 1]1 or of certain of these rights may be pronounced for a term of five to ten years.

(1)<L 2009-04-14/01, art. 9, 073; In force: 15-04-2009>
(2)<L 2014-05-05/09, art. 10, 106; In force: 18-07-2014>

Art. 135ter. (Repealed) <L 01-08-1979, art. 7>

Art. 135quater. <L 23-06-1961, art. unique> Is punished by imprisonment (from three months to two years), whoever obtains an engagement to serve in a foreign army or troop, from a minor not authorized to that effect by his parents, his guardian or his curator. <L 01-08-1979, art. 6>

Art. 135quinquies.<L 23-06-1961, art. unique> The attempt to commit the offences provided for [1 in article 135quater]1 shall be punished by the same penalties.

(1)<L 2014-05-05/09, art. 11, 106; In force: 18-07-2014>

PROVISION COMMON TO THIS TITLE.

###### Art. 136

Shall be exempted from the penalties imposed for conspiracies punished under this title, and for the offences provided for in article 111, those offenders who, before any attack and before any proceedings have commenced, shall have given the authority knowledge of such conspiracies or such offences, and of their authors or accomplices.

TITLE Ibis. - Serious violations of international humanitarian law. <inserted by L 2003-08-05/32, art. 6; In force: 07-08-2003>

Art. 136bis. <inserted by L 2003-08-05/32, art. 6; In force: 07-08-2003> Constitutes a crime under international law and is punished in accordance with the provisions of this title, the crime of genocide, as defined below, whether committed in time of peace or in time of war. In accordance with the Convention on the Prevention and Punishment of the Crime of Genocide of 9 December 1948, and without prejudice to the criminal provisions applicable to offences committed through negligence, the crime of genocide means any of the following acts committed with intent to destroy, in whole or in part, a national, ethnical, racial or religious group as such:
1° killing members of the group;
2° causing serious bodily or mental harm to members of the group; 3° deliberately inflicting on the group conditions of life calculated to bring about its physical destruction in whole or in part;
4° imposing measures intended to prevent births within the group; 5° forcibly transferring children of the group to another group.

Art. 136ter. <inserted by L 2003-08-05/32, art. 7; In force: 07-08-2003> Constitutes a crime under international law and is punished in accordance with the provisions of this title, the crime against humanity, as defined below, whether committed in time of peace or in time of war. In accordance with the Statute of the International Criminal Court, crime against humanity means any of the following acts when committed as part of a widespread or systematic attack directed against any civilian population, with knowledge of the attack:
1° murder;
2° extermination;
3° enslavement;
4° deportation or forcible transfer of population;
5° imprisonment or other severe deprivation of physical liberty in violation of fundamental rules of international law;
6° torture;
7° rape, sexual slavery, enforced prostitution, forced pregnancy, enforced sterilization, or any other form of sexual violence of comparable gravity;
8° persecution against any identifiable group or collectivity on political, racial, national, ethnic, cultural, religious, gender or

other grounds that are universally recognized as impermissible under international law, in connection with any act referred to in articles 136bis, 136ter and 136quater;
9° enforced disappearance of persons; 10° the crime of apartheid;
11° other inhumane acts of a similar character intentionally causing great suffering, or serious injury to body or to mental or physical health.

Art. 136quater. <inserted by L 2003-08-05/32, art. 8; In force: 07-08-2003> (NOTE: the third paragraph of article 136quater enters into force on the day of entry into force for Belgium of the Second Protocol to the Hague Convention of 1954 for the Protection of Cultural Property in the Event of Armed Conflict, adopted at The Hague on 26 March 1999; see L 2003-08-05/32, art. 29, §2)

###### § 1

Constitute crimes under international law and are punished in accordance with the provisions of this title, the war crimes referred to in the Conventions adopted at Geneva on 12 August 1949 and in Protocols I and II additional to those Conventions, adopted at Geneva on 8 June 1977, under the laws and customs applicable to armed conflicts, as defined in Article 2 of the Conventions adopted at Geneva on 12 August 1949, in Article 1 of Protocols I and II adopted at Geneva on 8 June 1977 additional to those Conventions, as well as in Article 8, § 2, f) of the Statute of the International Criminal Court, and enumerated hereinafter, when these crimes undermine, by action or omission, the protection of persons and property guaranteed respectively by those Conventions, Protocols, laws and customs, without prejudice to the criminal provisions applicable to offences committed through negligence:
1° wilful killing;
2° torture or other inhuman treatment, including biological experiments;
3° wilfully causing great suffering or serious injury to physical integrity or health;
4° rape, sexual slavery, enforced prostitution, forced pregnancy, enforced sterilization or any other form of sexual violence constituting a grave breach of the Geneva Conventions or a serious violation of Article 3 common to those Conventions;
5° other outrages upon human dignity, in particular humiliating and degrading

treatment;
6° compelling a prisoner of war, a civilian person protected by the Convention on the Protection of Civilian Persons in Time of War or a person protected in the same regard by Protocols I and II additional to the Geneva Conventions of 12 August 1949, to serve in the armed forces or armed groups of the enemy power or of the adverse party;
7° conscripting or enlisting children under the age of 15 years into armed forces or armed groups or using them to participate actively in hostilities;
8° depriving a prisoner of war, a civilian person protected by the Convention on the Protection of Civilian Persons in Time of War or a person protected in the same regard by Protocols I and II additional to the Geneva Conventions of 12 August 1949, of the right to a fair and regular trial as prescribed by those instruments;
9° unlawful deportation, transfer or displacement, unlawful confinement of a civilian person protected by the Convention on the Protection of Civilian Persons in Time of War or a person protected in the same regards by Protocols I and II additional to the Geneva Conventions of 12 August 1949;
10° intentionally using starvation of civilians as a method of warfare by depriving them of objects indispensable to their survival, including by wilfully impeding relief supplies as provided for under the Geneva Conventions;
11° taking of hostages;
12° destroying or seizing the property of an enemy, in the case of an international armed conflict, or of an adversary, in the case of an armed conflict not of an international character, unless such destruction or seizure be imperatively demanded by the necessities of war;
13° destruction and appropriation of property, not justified by military necessity as admitted under the law of nations and carried out on a large scale unlawfully and arbitrarily;
14° intentionally launching attacks against civilian objects, that is to say, objects which are not military objectives;
15° intentionally launching attacks against buildings, material, medical units and transport, and personnel using, in conformity with international law, the distinctive emblems provided for by international humanitarian law;
16° utilizing the presence of a civilian or other person protected by

international humanitarian law to render certain points, areas or military forces immune from military operations;
17° intentionally launching attacks against personnel, installations, material, units or vehicles involved in a humanitarian assistance or peacekeeping mission in accordance with the Charter of the United Nations, as long as they are entitled to the protection given to civilians and civilian objects under the international law of armed conflict;
18° acts and omissions, not legally justified, which are likely to endanger the health and physical or mental integrity of persons protected by international humanitarian law, in particular any medical procedure which is not justified by the state of health of such persons or is not consistent with generally recognized medical standards;
19° unless justified under the conditions provided for in 18°, acts consisting in carrying out on the persons referred to in 18°, even with their consent, physical mutilations, medical or scientific experiments or removal of tissue or organs for transplantation, unless it is a question of blood donations for transfusions or skin donations for grafts, provided that such donations are voluntary, consented to and intended for therapeutic purposes;
20° intentionally directing an attack against the civilian population or against individual civilians not taking direct part in hostilities;
21° intentionally launching an attack against places where the sick and wounded are collected provided that such places are not military objectives;
22° intentionally launching an attack in the knowledge that such attack will cause loss of life, injury to civilians or damage to civilian objects or widespread, long-term and severe damage to the natural environment which would be excessive in relation to the concrete and direct military advantage anticipated, without prejudice to the criminality of the attack whose harmful effects, even if proportionate to the expected military advantage, would be incompatible with the principles of the law of nations, as they result from established usage, the principles of humanity and the dictates of public conscience;
23° launching an attack against works or installations containing dangerous forces in the knowledge that such attack will cause loss of life, injury to civilians or damage to civilian objects which would be excessive in relation to the concrete and direct military advantage anticipated, without prejudice to the criminality of the attack whose harmful effects, even if proportionate to the expected military advantage, would be incompatible with the principles of the law of nations, as they result from established usage, the principles of humanity and the dictates of public conscience;
24° attacking or bombarding, by whatever means, demilitarized zones or towns, villages, dwellings or buildings which are undefended and which are not military objectives;
25° pillaging a town or place, even when taken by assault;
26° directing an attack against a person in the knowledge that the person is hors de combat on condition that such attack results in death or injury;
27° killing or wounding treacherously individuals belonging to the hostile nation or army or an adversary combatant;
28° declaring that no quarter will be given;
29° making improper use of the distinctive emblem of the red cross or the red crescent or other protective signs recognized by international humanitarian law, on condition that this act results in death or serious injury;
30° making improper use of a flag of truce, of the flag or of the military insignia and uniform of the enemy or of the United Nations, on condition that this act results in loss of life or serious injury;
31° the transfer, directly or indirectly, into occupied territory of parts of the civilian population of the occupying power, in the case of an international armed conflict, or of the occupying authority in the case of a non-international armed conflict;
32° unjustifiably delaying the repatriation of prisoners of war or civilians;
33° practising apartheid or other inhuman or degrading practices based on racial discrimination giving rise to outrages upon personal dignity;
34° directing attacks against historic monuments, works of art or places of worship which are clearly recognized and which constitute the cultural or spiritual heritage of peoples and to which special protection has been given by virtue of special arrangement when there is no evidence of violation by the adverse party of the prohibition on using such property in support of the military effort, and such property is not located in the immediate proximity

of military objectives;
35° intentionally launching attacks against buildings dedicated to religion, education, art, science or charitable purposes, historic monuments, hospitals, provided that such buildings are not military objectives;
36° employing poison or poisoned weapons;
37° employing asphyxiating, poisonous or other gases, and all analogous liquids, materials or devices;
38° employing bullets which expand or flatten easily in the human body, such as bullets with a hard envelope which does not entirely cover the core or is pierced with incisions;
39° declaring abolished, suspended or inadmissible in a court of law the rights and actions of persons belonging to the adverse party;
40° employing weapons, projectiles and material and methods of warfare of a nature to cause superfluous injury or unnecessary suffering or to strike indiscriminately in violation of the international law of armed conflict, provided that such weapons, projectiles, material and methods of warfare are the subject of a comprehensive prohibition and are included in an annex to the Statute of the International Criminal Court.

###### § 2

Constitute crimes under international law and are punished in accordance with the provisions of this title, serious violations of common Article 3 of the Conventions signed at Geneva on 12 August 1949, in the event of armed conflict as defined by that common Article 3, and enumerated below, when such violations infringe, by action or omission, the protection of persons guaranteed by those Conventions, without prejudice to the criminal provisions applicable to offences committed through negligence:
1° attacks on life and physical integrity, in particular murder in all its forms, mutilations, cruel treatment and torture;
2° attacks on the dignity of the person, in particular humiliating and degrading treatment;
3° the taking of hostages;
4° sentences passed and executions carried out without previous judgment rendered by a regularly constituted court, accompanied by judicial guarantees generally recognized as indispensable.

###### § 3

Constitute crimes under international law and are punished in accordance with the provisions of this title, the serious violations defined in

Article 15 of the Second Protocol to the Hague Convention of 1954 for the Protection of Cultural Property in the Event of Armed Conflict, adopted at The Hague on 26 March 1999, committed in the event of armed conflict, as defined in Article 18, §§ 1 and 2, of the Hague Convention of 1954 and in Article 22 of the aforementioned Second Protocol, and enumerated below, when such offences infringe, by action or omission, the protection of property guaranteed by those Convention and Protocol, without prejudice to the criminal provisions applicable to offences committed through negligence:
1° making a cultural property under enhanced protection the object of attack;
2° using a cultural property under enhanced protection or its immediate surroundings in support of military action;
3° destroying or appropriating on a large scale cultural property protected by the Convention and the Second Protocol.

Art. 136Quinquies.<inserted by L 2003-08-05/32, art. 9, In force: 07-08-2003> (NOTE: the last paragraph of article 136quinquies enters into force on the day of entry into force for Belgium of the Second Protocol to the Hague Convention of 1954 for the Protection of Cultural Property in the Event of Armed Conflict, adopted at The Hague on 26 March 1999; see L 2003-08-05/32, art. 29, §2) The offences enumerated in articles 136bis and 136ter are punished by life imprisonment.
The offences enumerated in 1°, 2°, 15°, 17°, 20° to 24° and 26° to 28° of paragraph 1 of article 136quater are punished by life imprisonment. The offences enumerated in 3°, 4°, 10°, 16°, 19°, 36° to 38° and 40° of the same paragraph of the same article are punished by imprisonment of twenty to thirty years. They are punished by life imprisonment if they have resulted in the death of one or more persons.
The offences enumerated in 12° to 14° and 25° of the same paragraph of the same article are punished by imprisonment of fifteen to twenty years. The same offence as well as that referred to in 29° and 30° of the same paragraph of the same article are punished by imprisonment of twenty to thirty years if they have resulted in either an apparently incurable illness, or [1 incapacity for personal work of more than four months]1, or the loss of absolute use of an organ or serious mutilation. They are punished by life imprisonment if they have resulted in the death of one or more persons.
The offences enumerated in 6° to 9°, 11° and 31° of the same paragraph of the same

article are punished by imprisonment of ten to fifteen years. In cases of aggravating circumstances provided for in the preceding paragraph, they are punished, as the case may be, by the penalties provided for in that paragraph.
The offences enumerated in 5° and 32° to 35° of the same paragraph of the same article are punished by imprisonment of ten to fifteen years, subject to the application of more severe criminal provisions punishing serious attacks on the dignity of the person.
The offence provided for in 18° of the same paragraph of the same article is punished by imprisonment of ten to fifteen years. It is punished by imprisonment of fifteen to twenty years when it has caused serious consequences for public health. The offence enumerated in 39° of the same paragraph of the same article is punished by imprisonment of ten to fifteen years.
The offence enumerated in 1° of paragraph 2 of article 136quater is punished by life imprisonment.
The offences enumerated in 2° and 4° of the same paragraph of the same article are punished by imprisonment of ten to fifteen years, subject to the application of more severe criminal provisions punishing serious attacks on the dignity of the person.
The offence enumerated in 3° of the same paragraph of the same article is punished by imprisonment of ten to fifteen years. The same offence is punished by imprisonment of twenty to thirty years if it has resulted in either an apparently incurable illness, or [1incapacity for personal work of more than four months]1, or the loss of absolute use of an organ, or serious mutilation. It is punished by life imprisonment if it has resulted in the death of one or more persons.
The offences enumerated in 1° to 3° of paragraph 3 of article 136quater are punished by imprisonment of fifteen to twenty years.

(1)<L 2016-02-05/11, art. 20, 114; In force: 29-02-2016>

Art. 136sexies. <inserted by L 2003-08-05/32, art. 10; In force: 07-08-2003> Those who manufacture, possess or transport an instrument, device or any object, erect a construction or transform an existing construction, knowing that the instrument, device, object, construction or transformation is intended to commit one of the offences provided for in articles 136bis, 136ter and 136quater or to facilitate its perpetration, are punished by the

penalty provided for the offence which they have enabled or facilitated the perpetration of.

Art. 136septies. <inserted by L 2003-08-05/32, art. 11; In force: 07-08-2003> Are punished by the penalty provided for the completed offence:
1° the order, even not followed by effect, to commit one of the offences provided for by articles 136bis, 136ter and 136quater;
2° the proposal or offer to commit such an offence and the acceptance of such proposal or offer;
3° the incitement to commit such an offence, even not followed by effect;
4° participation, within the meaning of articles 66 and 67, in such an offence, even not followed by effect;
5° the omission to act within the limits of their possibility of action on the part of those who had knowledge of orders given with a view to the execution of such an offence or of facts which begin its execution, and could have prevented its completion or put an end to it;
6° the attempt, within the meaning of articles 51 to 53, to commit such an offence.

Art. 136octies. <inserted by L 2003-08-05/32, art. 12; In force: 07-08-2003> § 1. Without prejudice to the exceptions set out in points 18°, 22° and 23° of article 136quater, § 1, no interest, no necessity of a political, military or national nature can justify the offences defined in articles 136bis, 136ter, 136quater, 136sexies and 136septies, even if they are committed by way of reprisals.

###### § 2

The fact that the accused acted on the order of his government or of a superior does not exempt him from his responsibility if, in the given circumstances, the order could clearly lead to the commission of one of the offences referred to in articles 136bis, 136ter and 136quater.

TITLE Iter. - TERRORIST OFFENCES. <Inserted by L 2003-12-19/34, art.
2; In force: 08-01-2004>


This excerpt sits under: CRIMINAL CODE > BOOK 2. - OFFENCES AND THEIR PUNISHMENT IN PARTICULAR > TITLE I. - CRIMES AND OFFENCES AGAINST THE SECURITY OF THE STATE > CHAPTER III. - CRIMES AGAINST THE INTERNAL SECURITY OF THE STATE

###### Art. 137

<Inserted by L 2003-12-19/34, art. 3, 046; In force: 08-01-2004> § 1. A terrorist offence is constituted by the offence provided for in §§ 2 and 3 which, by its nature or context, may seriously harm a country or an international organisation and is committed intentionally with the aim of seriously intimidating a population or unduly compelling public

authorities or an international organisation to perform or abstain from performing an act, or seriously destabilising or destroying the fundamental political, constitutional, economic or social structures of a country or an international organisation.

###### § 2. Under the conditions provided for in § 1, a terrorist offence is constituted by

1° intentional homicide or intentional assault and battery referred to in articles 393 to 404, 405bis, 405ter insofar as it refers to the aforementioned articles, 409, § 1, paragraph 1, and §§ 2 to 5, 410 insofar as it refers to the aforementioned articles, 417ter and 417quater;
2° hostage-taking referred to in article 347bis;
3° abduction referred to in articles 428 to 430, and 434 to 437;
4° massive destruction or damage referred to in articles 521, paragraphs 1 and 3, 522, 523, 525, 526, 550bis, § 3, 3°, in article 15 of the law of 5 June 1928 revising the Disciplinary and Penal Code for the merchant navy and maritime fisheries, as well as in article 114, § 4, of the law of 21 March 1991 on the reform of certain public economic enterprises, having the effect of endangering human lives or producing considerable economic losses;
5° the seizure of aircraft referred to in article 30, § 1, 2°, of the law of 27 June 1937 revising the law of 16 November 1919 on the regulation of air navigation;
6° the act of seizing by fraud, violence or threats against the captain of a ship, referred to in article 33 of the law of 5 June 1928 revising the Disciplinary and Penal Code for the merchant navy and maritime fisheries [1 as well as acts of piracy referred to in article 3 of the law of 30 December 2009 on the fight against maritime piracy]1;
7° the offences covered by the royal decree of 23 September 1958 establishing general regulations on the manufacture, storage, possession, sale, transport and use of explosive products, amended by the royal decree of 1 February 2000, and punished by articles 5 to 7 of the law of 28 May 1956 on explosive or deflagrable substances and mixtures and devices loaded with them;
8° the offences referred to in articles 510 to 513, 516 to 518, 520, 547 to 549, as well as in article 14 of the law of 5 June 1928 revising the Disciplinary and Penal Code for the merchant navy and maritime fisheries, having the effect of endangering human lives;

9° the offences covered by the law of [2 8 June 2006 regulating economic and individual activities with weapons]2;
10° the offences referred to in article 2, first paragraph, 2°, of the law of 10 July 1978 approving the Convention on the Prohibition of the Development, Production and Stockpiling of Bacteriological (Biological) and Toxin Weapons and on their Destruction, done at London, Moscow and Washington on 10 April 1972;
[2 11° the attempt, within the meaning of articles 51 to 53, to commit the offences referred to in this paragraph.]

###### §

3. Also constitutes, under the conditions provided for in § 1, a terrorist offence:
1° the massive destruction or damage, or the causing of a flood of an infrastructure, a transport system, public or private property, having the effect of endangering human lives or producing considerable economic losses, other than those referred to in § 2;
2° the seizure of means of transport other than those referred to in 5° and 6° of § 2;
3° the manufacture, possession, acquisition, transport or supply of nuclear or chemical weapons, the use of nuclear, biological or chemical weapons, as well as the research and development of chemical weapons;
4° the release of dangerous substances having the effect of endangering human lives;
5° the disruption or interruption of the supply of water, electricity or any other fundamental natural resource having the effect of endangering human lives;
6° the threat to commit one of the offences listed in § 2 or in this paragraph.

(1)<L 2009-12-30/12, art. 7, 076; In force: 14-01-2010>
(2)<L 2013-02-18/01, art. 2, 091; In force: 14-03-2013>

###### Art. 138

<Inserted by L 2003-12-19/34, art. 4, 046; In force: 08-01-2004> § 1. The penalties provided for the offences listed in article 137, § 2, are replaced as follows, if these offences constitute terrorist offences:
1° the fine, by imprisonment of one year to three years; 2° imprisonment of six months at most, by imprisonment of three years at most;

3° imprisonment of one year at most, by imprisonment of three years at most;
4° imprisonment of three years at most, by imprisonment of five years at most;
5° imprisonment of five years at most, by detention of five years to ten years;
6° detention of five years to ten years, by detention of ten years to fifteen years;
7° detention of ten years to fifteen years, by detention of fifteen years to twenty years; 8° detention of ten years to twenty years by detention of fifteen years to twenty years; 9° detention of fifteen years to twenty years, by detention of twenty years to thirty
years;
10° detention of twenty years to thirty years, by life detention.
[1 In the cases referred to in article 137, § 2, 11°, the maximum penalty provided for the completed offence shall be reduced by one year.]

###### §

2. The terrorist offences referred to in article 137, § 3, shall be punished by:
1° in the case referred to in 6°, imprisonment of three months to five years when the threat relates to an offence punishable by a correctional penalty, and detention of five years to ten years when the threat relates to an offence punishable by a criminal penalty;
2° detention of fifteen years to twenty years in the cases referred to in 1°, 2° and 5°; 3° life detention in the cases referred to in 3° and 4°.

(1)<L 2013-02-18/01, art. 3, 091; In force: 14-03-2013>

###### Art. 139

<Inserted by L 2003-12-19/34, art. 5, 046; In force: 08-01-2004> A terrorist group is constituted by the structured association of more than two persons, established over time, and which acts in a concerted manner with a view to committing terrorist offences referred to in article 137.
An organisation whose real object is exclusively of a political, trade union, philanthropic, philosophical or religious nature or which exclusively pursues any other legitimate aim cannot, as such, be considered a terrorist group within the meaning of paragraph 1.

###### Art. 140

<Inserted by L 2003-12-19/34, art. 6, 046; In force: 08-01-2004> § 1. Any person who participates in an activity of a terrorist group, including by providing information or material means to the terrorist group, or by

any form of financing of an activity of the terrorist group, [1 having had or having had to have knowledge]1 that this participation [1 could contribute]1 to committing a crime or an offence of the terrorist group, shall be punished by detention of five years to ten years and a fine of one hundred euros to five thousand euros.

###### § 2

Any leader of the terrorist group is liable to imprisonment of fifteen years to twenty years and a fine of one thousand euros to two hundred thousand euros.

(1)<L 2016-12-14/09, art. 2, 120; En vigueur : 01-01-2017>

Art. 140bis.[1 Without prejudice to the application of article 140, any person who disseminates or otherwise makes available to the public a message, with the intention of inciting [2 directly or indirectly]2 the commission of one of the offences referred to [2 in articles 137 or 140sexies]2, with the exception of that referred to in article 137, § 3, 6°, shall be punished by imprisonment of five years to ten years and a fine of one hundred euros to five thousand euros [2 ...]2.]1

(1)<Inséré par L 2013-02-18/01, art. 4, 091; En vigueur : 14-03-2013>
(2)<L 2016-08-03/15, art. 2, 119; En vigueur : 21-08-2016> (NOTE : par son arrêt n° 31/2018 du 15-03-2018 (M.B. 13-06-2018, p. 49144), la Cour constitutionnelle a annulé l'article 2, 3°)

Art. 140ter.[1 Without prejudice to the application of article 140, any person who recruits another person to commit one of the offences referred to [2 in articles 137, 140 or 140sexies]2, with the exception of that referred to in article 137, § 3, 6°, shall be punished by imprisonment of five years to ten years and a fine of one hundred euros to five thousand euros.]1

(1)<Inséré par L 2013-02-18/01, art. 5, 091; En vigueur : 14-03-2013>
(2)<L 2016-08-03/15, art. 3, 119; En vigueur : 21-08-2016>

Art. 140quater. [1 Without prejudice to the application of article 140, any person who provides instructions or training for the manufacture or use of explosives, firearms or other weapons or harmful or dangerous substances, or for other specific methods and techniques with a view to committing one of the offences referred to in article 137, with the exception of that referred to in article 137, § 3, 6°, shall be punished by imprisonment of five years to ten years and a

fine of one hundred euros to five thousand euros.]1

(1)<Inséré par L 2013-02-18/01, art. 6, 091; En vigueur : 14-03-2013>

Art. 140quinquies. [1 Without prejudice to the application of article 140, any person who, in Belgium or abroad, receives instructions or undergoes training referred to in article 140quater, with a view to committing one of the offences referred to in article 137, with the exception of that referred to in article 137, § 3, 6°, shall be punished by imprisonment of five years to ten years and a fine of one hundred euros to five thousand euros.]1

(1)	<Inséré par L 2013-02-18/01, art. 7, 091; En vigueur : 14-03-2013>

Art. 140sexies. [1 Without prejudice to the application of article 140, shall be punished by imprisonment of five years to ten years and a fine of one hundred euros to five thousand euros: 1° any person who leaves the national territory with a view to committing, in
Belgium or abroad, an offence referred to in articles 137, 140 to 140quinquies and 141, with the exception of the offence referred to in article 137, § 3, 6° ;
2° any person who enters the national territory with a view to committing, in Belgium or abroad, an offence referred to in articles 137, 140 to 140quinquies and 141, with the exception of the offence referred to in article 137, § 3, 6°.]1

(1)	<Inséré par L 2015-07-20/08, art. 2, 112; En vigueur : 15-08-2015>

Art. 140septies. [

###### § 1er. Any person who prepares the commission of a terrorist offence referred to in article 137, with the exception of the offence referred to in article 137, § 3, 6°, shall be punished :
-	by a term of imprisonment of eight days to one year, if the offence prepared is punishable by a term of imprisonment of five years at most;
-	by a term of imprisonment of three years at most, if the offence prepared is punishable by imprisonment of five years to ten years;
-	by a term of imprisonment of five years at most, if the offence prepared is punishable by imprisonment of ten years to fifteen years or by imprisonment of fifteen years to twenty years;
-	by imprisonment of five years to ten years, if the offence prepared is punishable by imprisonment of twenty years to thirty years or by life imprisonment.

The accessory penalties provided for the preparation are identical to those provided for the offence prepared.

###### § 2

For the application of this article, "prepare" means in particular : 1° collecting information concerning places, events or persons so as to be able to
commit an act in those places or during those events or to harm those persons, and observing those places, those events or those persons;
2° possessing, seeking, acquiring, transporting or manufacturing objects or substances likely to present a danger to others or to cause considerable economic losses;
3° possessing, seeking, acquiring, transporting or manufacturing financial or material means, false documents or illegally obtained documents, computer media, means of communication, means of transport;
4° possessing, seeking, acquiring premises that may serve as a retreat, a meeting place, a place of encounter or accommodation;
5° claiming in advance, in any form and by any means whatsoever, the commission of a terrorist offence, with the exception of the offence referred to in article 137, § 3, 6°.]1

(1)<Inséré par L 2016-12-14/09, art. 3, 120; En vigueur : 01-01-2017>

###### Art. 141

[1 Shall be punished by imprisonment of five years to ten years and a fine of one hundred euros to five thousand euros, any person who provides or collects, by any means whatsoever, directly or indirectly, material means, including financial assistance, with the intention that they be used or knowing that they will be used, in whole or in part,
1° with a view to committing or contributing to an offence referred to in articles 137 and 140 to 140septies;
or
2° by another person when the person who provides or collects the material means knows that that other person commits or will commit an offence referred to in article 137.]1

(1)<L 2016-12-14/09, art. 4, 120; En vigueur : 01-01-2017>

Art. 141bis. <Inséré par L 2003-12-19/34, art. 8; En vigueur : 08-01-2004> This title does not apply to the activities of armed forces during periods of armed conflict, as defined and governed by international humanitarian law, nor to activities undertaken by the armed forces of a State in the exercise of their official duties, insofar as they are governed by other rules of international law.

Art. 141ter.[1 No provision of this title may be interpreted as aiming to reduce or hinder [2 ...]2 fundamental rights or freedoms such as the right to strike, freedom of assembly and association, including the right to form and to join trade unions with others for the protection of one's interests, and the related right to demonstrate, freedom of expression, in particular freedom of the press and freedom of expression in other media, and as enshrined in particular in articles 8 to 11 of the European Convention for the Protection of Human Rights and Fundamental Freedoms.]1

(1)<L 2013-02-18/01, art. 8, 091; En vigueur : 14-03-2013>
(2)<L 2014-04-25/23, art. 2, 104; En vigueur : 24-05-2014>

### TITRE II

CRIMES AND OFFENCES WHICH INFRINGE RIGHTS GUARANTEED BY THE CONSTITUTION.

#### CHAPITRE I. - OFFENCES RELATING TO THE EXERCISE OF POLITICAL RIGHTS

<Abrogé par L 2003-12-19/34, art. 10, 046; En vigueur : 08-01-2004>

#### CHAPITRE I

OFFENCES RELATING TO THE FREE EXERCISE OF WORSHIP. <L 2003-12-
19/34, art. 11, 046; En vigueur : 08-01-2004>

###### Art. 142

Any person who, by violence or threats, has compelled or prevented one or more persons from practising a religion, attending the practice of that religion, celebrating certain religious festivals, observing certain days of rest, and, consequently, from opening or closing their workshops, shops or stores, and from doing or leaving certain work, shall be punished by imprisonment of eight days to two months and a fine of twenty-six [euros] to

two hundred [euros] <L 2000-06-26/42, art. 2, In force: 01-01-2002>.
[1 If the offence has been committed against a person whose situation of vulnerability by reason of age, a state of pregnancy, an illness, an infirmity or a physical or mental deficiency was apparent or known to the perpetrator, the latter shall be punished by imprisonment of fifteen days to six months and a fine of twenty-six euros to five hundred euros.]1

(1)<L 2011-11-26/19, art. 2, 084; In force: 02-02-2012>

###### Art. 143

Those who, by disturbances or disorders, have prevented, delayed or interrupted the practices of a religion which take place in a place intended for or habitually used for worship or in public ceremonies of that religion, shall be punished by imprisonment of eight days to three months and a fine of twenty-six [euros] to five hundred [euros] <L 2000-06-26/42, art. 2, In force: 01-01-2002>.

###### Art. 144

Any person who, by acts, words, gestures or threats, has outraged the objects of a religion, either in places intended for or habitually used for its practice, or in public ceremonies of that religion, shall be punished by imprisonment of fifteen days to six months and a fine of twenty-six [euros] to five hundred [euros] <L 2000-06-26/42, art. 2, In force: 01-01-2002>.

###### Art. 145

Shall be punished by the same penalties any person who, by acts, words, gestures or threats, has outraged a minister of a religion in the exercise of his ministry. If he has struck him, he shall be punished by imprisonment of two months to two years and a fine of fifty [euros] to five hundred [euros] <L 2000-06-26/42, art. 2, In force: 01-01-2002>.

###### Art. 146

If the blows have caused an effusion of blood, a wound or an illness, the offender shall be punished by imprisonment of six months to five years and a fine of one hundred [euros] to one thousand [euros] <L 2000-06-26/42, art. 2, In force
: 01-01-2002>.

#### CHAPITRE II

INFRINGEMENTS BY PUBLIC OFFICIALS OF RIGHTS GUARANTEED BY THE CONSTITUTION. <L 2003-12-19/34, art. 12, 046; In
force: 08-01-2004>

###### Art. 147

Any public official or officer, any depositary or agent of authority or of the public force, who has unlawfully and arbitrarily arrested or caused to be arrested, detained or caused to be detained one or more persons, shall be punished by imprisonment of three months to two years.
The imprisonment shall be six months to three years, if the unlawful and arbitrary detention has lasted more than ten days.
If it has lasted more than one month, the offender shall be sentenced to imprisonment of one year to five years.
He shall, in addition, be punished by a fine of fifty [euros] to one thousand [euros] and may be sentenced to disqualification from the rights indicated in nos. 1, 2 and 3 of [1 article 31, paragraph 1]1. <L 2000-06-26/42, art. 2, In force: 01-01-2002>

(1)<L 2009-04-14/01, art. 10, 073; In force: 15-04-2009>

###### Art. 148

Any official of the administrative or judicial order, any officer of justice or police, any commander or agent of the public force, who, acting in that capacity, has entered the dwelling of an inhabitant against the latter's will, outside the cases provided for and without the formalities prescribed by law, shall be punished by imprisonment of eight days to six months and a fine of twenty-six [euros] to two hundred [euros] <L 2000-06-26/42, art. 2, In force: 01-01-2002>.

###### Art. 149. (Repealed)

<L 26-12-1956, art. 36>

###### Art. 150. (Repealed)

<L 13-10-1930, art. 31>

###### Art. 151

Any other arbitrary act infringing the liberties and rights guaranteed by the Constitution, ordered or executed by a public official or officer, by a depositary or agent of authority or of the public force, shall be punished by imprisonment of fifteen days to one year.

###### Art. 152

If the accused proves that he acted by order of his superiors, for matters within their competence and in respect of which hierarchical obedience was owed to them, the penalties provided for by the preceding articles shall be applied only to the superiors who gave the order.

###### Art. 153

If the public officials or officers, accused of having ordered, authorised or facilitated one of the acts mentioned in articles 148 to 151, claim that their signature was obtained by fraud, they shall be required, while causing the act to cease if applicable, to denounce the offender; otherwise, they shall be prosecuted personally.

###### Art. 154. (See NOTE under TITLE)

If one of the arbitrary acts mentioned in articles 148 to 151 has been committed by means of the false signature of a public official, the perpetrators of the forgery and those who, maliciously or fraudulently, have made use of it, shall be punished (by imprisonment) of ten years to fifteen years. <L 2003-01-23/42, art. 40, 040; In force: 13-03-2003>

###### Art. 155

Public officials or officers responsible for administrative or judicial police, who, having the power, have neglected or refused to cause an unlawful detention brought to their knowledge to cease, shall be punished by imprisonment of one month to one year.

###### Art. 156
